### [Mobile Offshore Unit Certificates]{.HTitleText2} {#h-730337 .Subheading}

#### [Offshore Installation Manager, MOU/surface]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730338 .Subheading}

**[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for an
Offshore Installation Manager, MOU/surface certificate shall meet the
requirements set out in column 1 of the table to this section and the
corresponding specifications set out in column 2.

<figure id="730340">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th rowspan="2" class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Item</th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
<th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Requirements</th>
<th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Specifications</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Experience</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><p>At least 42 months of qualifying service,
including assisting in at least 20 cargo transfer operations at sea
between an MOU and a supply vessel, assisting in at least 4 complete
relocation moves of an MOU/surface and assisting in at least
20 helicopter landings on and departures from an MOU, which service
shall comprise the following:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> at least 9 months as a barge
supervisor, maintenance supervisor, toolpusher, tourpusher, chief mate
or equivalent to chief mate, on board one or more MOUs/surface;
and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> any remaining time in any
position specified in Chapter 3 of TP 2293 on board one or more
MOUs.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Certificates, or their approved
equivalents, to be provided to the examiner</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><p>The applicant shall provide the following
certificates or, as an alternative, in the case of each certificate
referred to in paragraphs (a) to (f), a certificate referred to in the
Canadian East Coast Offshore Petroleum Industry, Standard Practice for
the Training and Qualification of Personnel, published by the Canadian
Association of Petroleum Producers, if the Minister has determined it to
be equivalent to the relevant certificate:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> MED with respect to STCW
basic safety;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> MED training with respect to
proficiency in survival craft and rescue boats other than fast rescue
boats;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> MED in advanced fire
fighting;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(d)</span> MED for senior officers or
the Command and Control and Management of Major Emergencies training
course described in TP 10937;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(e)</span> MOU courses described in
TP 10937 with respect to</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> basic offshore
survival,</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> stability and ballast
control, MOU/surface,</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(iii)</span> hydrogen sulphide
(H<sub>2</sub>S) training, and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(iv)</span> supervisor, offshore well
control;</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(f)</span> marine advanced first aid or
a certificate with respect to a course that the Minister accepts as
being equivalent in content to the marine advanced first aid course;
and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(g)</span> meteorology if the applicant
has not passed the written examination referred to in
paragraph 3(b).</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight">3</td>
<td class="noBorderRight">Pass examinations</td>
<td><ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> Navigation safety,
level 1;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> meteorology, level 2 if the
certificate referred to in paragraph 2(g) is not provided; and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> oral examination on general
seamanship with respect to an MOU/surface, after meeting the other
requirements of this table.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
</figure>

#### [Offshore Installation Manager, MOU/self-elevating]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730346 .Subheading}

**[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for an
Offshore Installation Manager, MOU/self-elevating certificate shall meet
the requirements set out in column 1 of the table to this section and
the corresponding specifications set out in column 2.

<figure id="730348">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th rowspan="2" class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Item</th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
<th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Requirements</th>
<th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Specifications</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Experience</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><p>At least 42 months of service, including
assisting in at least 20 cargo transfer operations at sea between an MOU
and a supply vessel, assisting in at least 4 complete relocation moves
of an MOU/self-elevating and assisting in at least 20 helicopter
landings on and departures from an MOU, which service shall comprise the
following:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> at least 9 months as a barge
supervisor, maintenance supervisor, toolpusher, tourpusher, chief mate
or equivalent to chief mate, on one or more vessels that are
MOU/self-elevating; and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> any remaining time in any
position specified in Chapter 3 of TP 2293 on one or more MOUs.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Certificates, or their approved
equivalents, to be provided to the examiner</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><p>The applicant shall provide the following
certificates or, as an alternative, in the case of each certificate
referred to in paragraphs (a) to (f), a certificate referred to in the
Canadian East Coast Offshore Petroleum Industry, Standard Practice for
the Training and Qualification of Personnel, published by the Canadian
Association of Petroleum Producers, if the Minister has determined it to
be equivalent to the relevant certificate:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> MED with respect to STCW
basic safety;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> MED training with respect to
proficiency in survival craft and rescue boats other than fast rescue
boats;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> MED in advanced fire
fighting;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(d)</span> MED for senior officers or
the Command and Control and Management of Major Emergencies training
course described in TP 10937;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(e)</span> MOU courses set out in
TP 10937 with respect to</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> basic offshore
survival,</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> stability,
MOU/self-elevating,</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(iii)</span> hydrogen sulphide
(H<sub>2</sub>S) training, and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(iv)</span> supervisor, offshore well
control;</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(f)</span> marine advanced first aid;
and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(g)</span> meteorology if the applicant
has not passed the written examination referred to in
paragraph 3(b).</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight">3</td>
<td class="noBorderRight">Pass examinations</td>
<td><ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> Navigation safety,
level 1;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> meteorology, level 2 if the
certificate referred to in paragraph 2(g) is not provided; and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> oral examination on general
seamanship with respect to an MOU/self-elevating, after meeting the
other requirements of this table.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
</figure>

#### [Barge Supervisor, MOU/surface]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730354 .Subheading}

**[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for a
Barge Supervisor, MOU/surface certificate shall meet the requirements
set out in column 1 of the table to this section and the corresponding
specifications set out in column 2.

<figure id="730356">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th rowspan="2" class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Item</th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
<th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Requirements</th>
<th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Specifications</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Hold a certificate</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom">Master, Near Coastal.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Experience</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><p>At least 12 months of service, including
assisting in at least 10 cargo transfer operations at sea between an
MOU/surface and a supply vessel, assisting in at least 2 complete
relocation moves of an MOU/surface and assisting in at least
10 helicopter landings on and departures from an MOU, which service
shall comprise the following:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> at least 6 months as a
watchkeeping mate on board one or more MOUs/surface while holding a
Master, Near Coastal certificate; and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> any remaining time made up of
any combination of service as a driller, a watchkeeping mate, an
engineer, a maintenance supervisor or a toolpusher.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">3</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Certificates, or their approved
equivalents, to be provided to the examiner</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><p>The applicant shall provide the following
certificates or, as an alternative, in the case of each certificate
referred to in paragraphs (a) to (f), a certificate referred to in the
Canadian East Coast Offshore Petroleum Industry, Standard Practice for
the Training and Qualification of Personnel, published by the Canadian
Association of Petroleum Producers, if the Minister has determined it to
be equivalent to the relevant certificate:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> MED with respect to STCW
basic safety;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> MED training with respect to
proficiency in survival craft and rescue boats other than fast rescue
boats;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> MED in advanced fire
fighting;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(d)</span> MED for senior officers or
the Command and Control and Management of Major Emergencies training
course described in TP 10937;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(e)</span> MOU courses described in
TP 10937 with respect to</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> basic offshore
survival,</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> stability and ballast
control, MOU/surface, and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(iii)</span> hydrogen sulphide
(H<sub>2</sub>S) training; and</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(f)</span> marine advanced first
aid.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight">4</td>
<td class="noBorderRight">Pass an examination</td>
<td>Oral examination on general seamanship with respect to an
MOU/surface, after meeting the other requirements of this table.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
</figure>

#### [Barge Supervisor, MOU/self-elevating]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730362 .Subheading}

**[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for a
Barge Supervisor, MOU/self-elevating certificate shall meet the
requirements set out in column 1 of the table to this section and the
corresponding specifications set out in column 2.

<figure id="730364">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th rowspan="2" class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Item</th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
<th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Requirements</th>
<th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Specifications</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Experience</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><p>At least 12 months of service made up of
any combination of service as a toolpusher, a nightpusher, a crane
operator, a derrickman, a driller, an assistant driller, a barge
supervisor, a barge supervisor trainee, a tourpusher, or an engineering
watchkeeper, which service shall include assisting in</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> at least 10 cargo transfer
operations at sea between an MOU/self-elevating and a supply
vessel;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> at least 2 complete
relocation moves of an MOU/self-elevating; and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> at least 10 helicopter
landings on and departures from an MOU.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Certificates, or their approved
equivalents, to be provided to the examiner</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><p>The applicant shall provide the following
certificates or, as an alternative, in the case of each certificate
referred to in paragraphs (a) to (f), a certificate referred to in the
Canadian East Coast Offshore Petroleum Industry, Standard Practice for
the Training and Qualification of Personnel, published by the Canadian
Association of Petroleum Producers, if the Minister has determined it to
be equivalent to the relevant certificate:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> MED with respect to STCW
basic safety;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> MED training with respect to
proficiency in survival craft and rescue boats other than fast rescue
boats;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> MED in advanced fire
fighting;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(d)</span> MED for senior officers or
the Command and Control and Management of Major Emergencies training
course described in TP 10937;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(e)</span> MOU courses described in
TP 10937 with respect to</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> basic offshore
survival,</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> stability,
MOU/self-elevating, and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(iii)</span> hydrogen sulphide
(H<sub>2</sub>S) training;</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(f)</span> marine advanced first aid;
and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(g)</span> meteorology if the applicant
has not passed the written examination referred to in
paragraph 3(b).</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight">3</td>
<td class="noBorderRight">Pass examinations</td>
<td><ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> Navigation safety,
level 1;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> meteorology, level 2 if the
certificate referred to in paragraph 2(g) has not been provided;
and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> oral examination on general
seamanship with respect to an MOU/self-elevating, after meeting the
other requirements of this table.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
</figure>

#### [Maintenance Supervisor, MOU/surface]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730370 .Subheading}

**[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for a
Maintenance Supervisor, MOU/surface certificate shall meet the
requirements set out in column 1 of the table to this section and the
corresponding specifications set out in column 2.

<figure id="730372">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th rowspan="2" class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Item</th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
<th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Requirements</th>
<th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Specifications</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Hold a certificate</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom">Fourth-class Engineer, Motor Ship</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Experience</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> If holding a First-class
Engineer, Motor Ship certificate, acquire at least 3 months of
qualifying service performing engineering duties on board one or more
MOUs/surface after having obtained a Second-class Engineer, Motor Ship
certificate;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> if holding a Second-class
Engineer, Motor Ship certificate, acquire at least 12 months of
qualifying service performing engineering duties on board one or more
MOUs/surface after having obtained a Fourth-class Engineer, Motor Ship
certificate;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> if holding a Third-class
Engineer, Motor Ship certificate, acquire at least 24 months of
qualifying service performing engineering duties on board one or more
MOUs/surface after having obtained a Fourth-class Engineer, Motor Ship
certificate;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(d)</span> if holding a Fourth-class
Engineer, Motor Ship certificate, acquire at least 36 months of
qualifying service performing engineering duties on board one or more
MOUs/surface after having obtained that certificate; and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(e)</span> if holding a Maintenance
Supervisor, MOU/self-elevating certificate, acquire at least 12 months
of qualifying service performing engineering duties on board one or more
MOUs/surface after having obtained that certificate.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">3</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Certificates, or their approved
equivalents, to be provided to the examiner</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><p>The applicant shall provide the following
certificates or, as an alternative, in the case of each certificate
referred to in paragraphs (<em>a</em>) to (f), a certificate referred to
in the Canadian East Coast Offshore Petroleum Industry, Standard
Practice for the Training and Qualification of Personnel, published by
the Canadian Association of Petroleum Producers, if the Minister has
determined it to be equivalent to the relevant certificate:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> MED with respect to STCW
basic safety;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> MED training with respect to
proficiency in survival craft and rescue boats other than fast rescue
boats;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> MED in advanced fire
fighting;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(d)</span> MED for senior officers or
the Command and Control and Management of Major Emergencies training
course described in TP 10937;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(e)</span> MOU courses described in
TP 10937 with respect to</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> basic offshore survival,
and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> hydrogen sulphide
(H<sub>2</sub>S) training;</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(f)</span> marine advanced first aid;
and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(g)</span> ship management practices
taught using a propulsive plant simulator.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight">4</td>
<td class="noBorderRight">Pass examinations</td>
<td><ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> Written examinations</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> at the second-class engineer
level, on technical drawing, and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> at the first-class engineer
level,</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(A)</span> on applied
mechanics,</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(B)</span> on thermodynamics,</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(C)</span> on electrotechnology,
and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(D)</span> on naval
architecture;</p></li>
</ul></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> examination on ship
management practices using a propulsive plant simulator, after providing
the certificate referred to in paragraph 3(g);</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> written examination on
general engineering knowledge at the first-class engineer level, after
meeting the requirements of items 2 and 3 and paragraphs (a) and
(b);</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(d)</span> written examination on
engineering knowledge of motor vessels at the first-class engineer
level, after meeting the requirements of paragraph (<em>c</em>);
and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(e)</span> oral examination with respect
to an MOU/surface, after meeting the requirements of
paragraph (d).</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
</figure>

#### [Maintenance Supervisor, MOU/self-elevating]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730378 .Subheading}

**[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for a
Maintenance Supervisor, MOU/self-elevating certificate shall meet the
requirements set out in column 1 of the table to this section and the
corresponding specifications set out in column 2.

<figure id="730380">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th rowspan="2" class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Item</th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 1</th>
<th class="noBorderBottom top" scope="col">Column 2</th>
</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Requirements</th>
<th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Specifications</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Experience</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> at least 12 months of
qualifying service on board one or more MOUs/self-elevating after having
obtained a Third-class Engineer, Motor Ship certificate;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> if the applicant holds one of
the following certificates, at least 24 months of qualifying service on
board one or more MOUs/self-elevating after obtaining that
certificate:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> a certificate issued by a
technical college recognized by a province based on an accredited 2-year
course in the electrical, instrumentation or mechanical trades,
or</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> a Canadian inter-provincial
journey person’s certificate in the electrical, instrumentation or
mechanical trades;</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> at least 60 months of
qualifying service on board one or more MOUs/self-elevating; or</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(d)</span> at least 3 months of
qualifying service on board one or more MOUs/self-elevating after having
obtained a Maintenance Supervisor, MOU/surface certificate.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">2</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Certificates, or their approved
equivalents, to be provided to the examiner</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom"><p>The applicant shall provide the following
certificates or, as an alternative, in the case of each certificate
referred to in paragraphs (a) to (f), a certificate referred to in the
Canadian East Coast Offshore Petroleum Industry, Standard Practice for
the Training and Qualification of Personnel, published by the Canadian
Association of Petroleum Producers, if the Minister has determined it to
be equivalent to the relevant certificate:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> MED with respect to STCW
basic safety;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> MED training with respect to
proficiency in survival craft and rescue boats other than fast rescue
boats;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> MED in advanced fire
fighting;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(d)</span> MED for senior officers or
the Command and Control and Management of Major Emergencies training
course described in TP 10937;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(e)</span> MOU courses described in
TP 10937 with respect to</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> basic offshore survival,
and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> hydrogen sulphide
(H<sub>2</sub>S) training;</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(f)</span> marine advanced first aid;
and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(g)</span> ship watchkeeping practices
taught using a propulsive plant simulator.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight">3</td>
<td class="noBorderRight">Pass examinations</td>
<td><ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> The following examinations at
the third-class engineer level, after meeting the requirements of
items 1 and 2:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(i)</span> general engineering
knowledge,</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(ii)</span> engineering knowledge of
motor vessels, and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(iii)</span> if the applicant does not
hold a certificate referred to in paragraph 1(b), electrotechnology;
and</p></li>
</ul></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> oral examination with respect
to an MOU/self-elevating, after meeting the requirements of
paragraph (a).</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
</figure>

#### [Ballast Control Operator]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730386 .Subheading}

**[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for a
Ballast Control Operator certificate shall meet the requirements set out
in column 1 of the table to this section and the corresponding
specifications set out in column 2.

<figure id="730388">
<table
class="table table-hover table-striped tablePointsize8 topdouble topbot">
<thead>
<tr class="topdouble">
<th rowspan="2" class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Item</th>

</tr>
<tr>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom"
scope="col">Requirements</th>
<th class="borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Specifications</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">Experience</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom">At least 6 months as a ballast control
operator trainee.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight">2</td>
<td class="noBorderRight">Certificates, or their approved equivalents,
to be provided to the examiner</td>
<td><p>The applicant shall provide the following certificates with
respect to MOU courses described in TP 10937 or, as an alternative, in
the case of each certificate referred to in paragraphs (a) to (c), a
certificate referred to in the Canadian East Coast Offshore Petroleum
Industry, Standard Practice for the Training and Qualification of
Personnel, published by the Canadian Association of Petroleum Producers,
if the Minister has determined it to be equivalent to the relevant
certificate:</p>
<ul>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(a)</span> basic offshore
survival;</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(b)</span> stability and ballast
control, (MOU/surface); and</p></li>
<li><p><span class="tablelabel">(c)</span> hydrogen sulphide
(H<sub>2</sub>S) training.</p></li>
</ul></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

</figure>

#### [Transitional Period]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730394 .Subheading}

- **[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  applicant who has started to acquire qualifying service and who has
  passed at least one examination required to obtain a certificate of
  competency under the [Marine Certification
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-97-391) before that day may, after
  complying with the requirements for the delivery of that certificate
  under those Regulations as they read immediately before that day,
  obtain the equivalent ranking certificate identified in Schedule 1 to
  this Part and issued under these Regulations or, if an equivalent
  ranking certificate does not exist, the certificate of the rank
  immediately below.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) expires 5 years after the day on which
  this section comes into force.

**\[185 to 199 reserved\]**

:::: {#730399 .Schedule}

for exchange or additional requirements applicable to first
renewals</th>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Corresponding
certificate for exchange and, as the case may be, renewal of the
certificate</th>
<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">STCW
endorsement</th>

</tr>

<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">1</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom" scope="row">Master mariner
(issued before the coming into force of this Schedule)</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">See note 1.</td>

106</p></figcaption>

- [Note 1:]{.lawlabel} An applicant who does not have an STCW 95
  endorsement shall provide to the examiner training certificates with
  respect to the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} SEN Level II, completed after September 1, 1989, or
    ARPA; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} MED for senior officers.

- [Note 2:]{.lawlabel} An applicant shall provide to the examiner a
  training certificate in SEN Level I, completed after September 1,
  1989, or ARPA.

- [Note 3:]{.lawlabel} An applicant shall provide to the examiner the
  following training certificates:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} MED training with respect to proficiency in
    survival craft and rescue boats other than fast rescue boats; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} MED in advanced fire fighting.

- [Note 4:]{.lawlabel} An applicant shall provide to the examiner a
  training certificate with respect to MED for senior officers.

- [Note 5:]{.lawlabel} In respect of a tug, a non-STCW endorsement
  limited to a tug of up to 3 000 gross tonnage that is engaged on a
  limited, contiguous waters voyage.

- [Note 6:]{.lawlabel} Renewal of the certificate in accordance with the
  requirements of section 105.

- [Note 7:]{.lawlabel} Renewal of the certificate for limited,
  contiguous waters voyages, subject to any other limitations on the
  certificate before its renewal.

- [Note 8:]{.lawlabel} If the certificate to be exchanged has an STCW 95
  endorsement, the examination requirements set out in column 2 do not
  apply.

## [SCHEDULE 2 TO PART 1]{.scheduleLabel}[(Paragraph 110(2)(a))]{.OriginatingRef}[Application To Be Examined --- Information To Be Provided and Required Declarations]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-730420 .scheduleLabel}
:::

- [1]{.lawlabel} Information on the applicant:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} name

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} candidate number (CDN)

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} date of birth

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} home address and telephone number

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} civil status

- [2]{.lawlabel} Certificate or endorsement applied for

- [3]{.lawlabel} Place and date where, and time when, the examination is
  to be held

- [4]{.lawlabel} Examination the applicant wishes to undertake

- [5]{.lawlabel} The following declaration signed and dated by the
  applicant:

  "I hereby declare that, to the best of my knowledge and belief, the
  particulars contained in this application are correct and that the
  supporting documents and testimonials submitted along with it are true
  and genuine documents signed by the persons whose names appear on
  them."

- [6]{.lawlabel} The following declaration signed and dated by the
  examiner:

  "I hereby certify that the above particulars are correct and that the
  applicant has produced all necessary documents and testimonials in
  support of this application."

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[Crewing]{.HTitleText1} {#h-730496 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-730497 .Subheading}

#### [Application]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730498 .Subheading}

- **[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Division 2 applies in respect of
  self-propelled Canadian vessels, other than cable ferries to which
  only section 205 applies, that are engaged on a voyage.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Division 3 applies in respect of MOUs that are
  Canadian vessels, on station for the purpose of petroleum exploration
  or production, except MOUs in the Great Lakes Basin or waters to which
  the Canada -- Nova Scotia Offshore Petroleum Resources Accord
  Implementation Act or the [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic
  Accord Implementation Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5) applies.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Division 4 applies in respect of foreign vessels that
  are in Canadian waters except MOUs in waters to which the Canada Nova
  Scotia Offshore Petroleum Resources Accord Implementation Act or the
  [Canada--Newfoundland and Labrador Atlantic Accord Implementation
  Act](/eng/acts/C-7.5) applies.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Division 5 applies in respect of all vessels that are
  not laid up and that are securely

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} anchored in port; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} moored to shore.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Division 6 applies in respect of Canadian vessels
  that are special design craft such as high-speed crafts, air cushion
  vessels, wing-in-ground crafts, passenger submersible crafts, sail
  training vessels and any other sailing vessels.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Division 7 applies in respect of vessels that are not
  securely anchored in port or securely moored to shore and that are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Canadian vessels that are required to be fitted
    with a radio installation under the [Navigation Safety Regulations,
    2020](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-216); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} foreign vessels in Canadian waters that are fitted
    with a ship station.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} Division 8 applies in respect of seafarers who are or
  who seek to be members of the complement of a Canadian vessel and who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are or would be required by this Part to hold a
    certificate issued under

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Part 1, except under section 138, 143, 151 or 174
      or, in the case of a vessel that is not a passenger-carrying
      vessel, section 131, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the [Canada Shipping Act](/eng/acts/S-9);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are or would be employed by the authorized
    representative to work on board a vessel of at least 200 gross
    tonnage, other than a fishing vessel, that is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} engaged in a commercial operation, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} engaged on an unlimited voyage or a near coastal
      voyage, Class 1;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} are or would be employed by the authorized
    representative to work on board a vessel, other than a fishing
    vessel, that is engaged on an international voyage other than an
    inland voyage; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if an application has been made under section 334
    or 335 in respect of a fishing vessel, is or would be employed by
    the authorized representative to work on board a fishing vessel that
    is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} engaged in a commercial operation and on an
      unlimited voyage or a near coastal voyage, Class 1, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} engaged on an international voyage.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2014, c. 13, s. 117
- [SOR/2020-216, s. 414](#1263794-1264126){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}

:::

#### [General Prohibition]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730523 .Subheading}

**[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** No Canadian vessel
shall navigate anywhere and no foreign vessel shall navigate in Canadian
waters unless the requirements of this Part are met.

#### [Safe Manning Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730525 .Subheading}

- **[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a Safety Convention vessel shall ensure
  that the vessel meets the safe manning requirements established for
  the vessel by the Administration in accordance with IMO Resolution
  A.890(21), Principles of Safe Manning, or any other resolution that
  replaces it.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If one of the safe manning requirements established
  in accordance with subsection (1) sets out that a person shall hold a
  certificate, the certificate shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} issued or endorsed by the Administration; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} endorsed as meeting the requirements of the STCW
    Convention.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a Canadian vessel
  shall apply to the Minister for the following document and the
  Minister shall issue the document following that application:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Safety Convention vessel, a Safe
    Manning Document that complies with IMO Resolution A.890(21),
    Principles of Safe Manning, or any other resolution that replaces
    it; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel that is not a Safety
    Convention vessel and that is required to carry an inspection
    certificate, a Safe Manning Document, valid for a maximum of 5 years
    after the day of its issuance, that specifies

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the minimum number of members of the complement,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the certificates required to be held by the
      members of the complement,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any endorsements, conditions or limitations on
      the certificates referred to in subparagraph (ii),

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the voyages that the vessel is authorized to
      engage on, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, the number of passengers that the
      vessel is authorized to have on board.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (3)(*b*) does not apply until the later of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date of the next periodical inspection of the
    vessel, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 1 year after the day on which this section comes
    into force.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a Canadian Safety
  Convention vessel or a vessel that is required to carry an inspection
  certificate shall ensure that the Safe Manning Document issued by the
  Minister for that vessel under subsection (3) is carried on board.

#### [Inspection of Certificates and Endorsements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730543 .Subheading}

**[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** The master of a vessel
or the offshore installation manager of an MOU to which Division 3
applies shall ensure that the certificates and endorsements required by
these Regulations are kept readily available on board the vessel for
inspection by a marine safety inspector.

#### [Dispensations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730545 .Subheading}

**[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** If no person holding a
certificate or an endorsement required by these Regulations is available
to occupy a position in respect of which that certificate or endorsement
is necessary, the Minister may, in accordance with Article VIII of the
STCW Convention, grant a dispensation permitting a specified person to
serve in that position on board a specified vessel until the earlier of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which a holder of that certificate or
  endorsement is available, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} 6 months after the date on which the dispensation is
  granted.

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Canadian Vessels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-730549 .Subheading}

#### [Training and Familiarization]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730550 .Subheading}

- **[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master and the authorized representative of a vessel shall ensure that
  any person assigned a function on that vessel receives the on-board
  familiarization and safety training set out in TP 4957 before they
  start to perform any duty on board the vessel.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and (4), the master and
  the authorized representative of a vessel shall ensure that every
  member of the complement who is required to be on board in order for
  the vessel to meet the safe manning requirements of this Part holds a
  training certificate in marine emergency duties with respect to STCW
  basic safety.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The master and the authorized representative of a
  vessel described in column 1 of the table to this subsection shall
  ensure that every member of the complement who is required to be on
  board in order for the vessel to meet the safe manning requirements of
  this Part obtains, before acquiring a total of 6 months of sea
  service, at least one of the training certificates in marine emergency
  duties set out as applicable in columns 2 to 4 or, if applicable, the
  Pleasure Craft Operator Card referred to in column 5.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The master and the authorized representative of a
  passenger-carrying vessel that is not a ferry, is engaged on a
  sheltered water voyage, operates only on a seasonal basis between
  March 31 and December 1 of each year and has unberthed accommodations
  shall ensure that every member of the complement who is required to be
  on board in order for the vessel to meet the safe manning requirements
  of this Part obtains, before acquiring a total of 6 months of sea
  service,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a training certificate in marine emergency duties
    with respect to small seasonal passenger vessel safety
    (non-certificated personnel) for that vessel, in the case of a
    person who is not required by these Regulations to hold a
    certificate to perform their duties on board the vessel; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in all other cases, at least one of the training
    certificates in marine emergency duties with respect to basic safety
    that are set out as applicable in columns 2 to 4 of item 6 of the
    table to subsection (3).

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The master and the authorized representative of a
  vessel shall ensure that every person assigned to a fire team on the
  muster list holds

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is engaged on a sheltered waters
    voyage and is required to carry a fireman's outfit, a training
    certificate in marine emergency duties with respect to basic safety;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is engaged on a sheltered waters
    voyage and is not required to carry a fireman's outfit,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one of the training certificates in marine
      emergency duties set out in columns 2 to 4 of the table to
      subsection (3), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel operates only on a seasonal basis
      between March 31 and December 1 of each year, a training
      certificate in marine emergency duties with respect to small
      seasonal passenger vessel safety (non-certificated personnel) for
      that vessel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in all other cases, except those set out in the
    table to subsection (3), a training certificate in marine emergency
    duties with respect to STCW basic safety.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} The master and the authorized representative of a
  vessel shall ensure that every person assigned on the muster list to
  the preparation or launching of a survival craft, other than a
  manually launched life raft or manually launched inflatable rescue
  platform, holds a training certificate in marine emergency duties with
  respect to proficiency in survival craft and rescue boats other than
  fast rescue boats.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} The master and the authorized representative of a
  vessel that is equipped with a marine evacuation system (MES) shall
  ensure that every person employed on board the vessel has received
  training regarding the deployment of the system using the vessel's
  on-board training aids, and that persons assigned on the muster list
  to the operation of the MES have

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} participated in a full deployment of the system in
    accordance with the system's approval certificate; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provided an attestation evidencing successful
    completion of the manufacturer's training.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} The master and the authorized representative shall
  ensure that a person designated to take charge of medical care on
  board a vessel

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is a physician; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has successfully completed the approved training
    course in marine medical care.

- [(9)]{.lawlabel} The master and the authorized representative of a
  vessel shall ensure that a person designated to provide first aid on
  board the vessel holds

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
    Class 2 or a sheltered waters voyage, a training certificate in
    marine basic or advanced first aid or, in the case of an MOU, a
    certificate with respect to a course that the Minister accepts as
    being equivalent in content to the marine advanced first aid course;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is engaged on an unlimited voyage or
    a near coastal voyage, Class 1, a training certificate in marine
    advanced first aid or, in the case of an MOU, a certificate with
    respect to a course that the Minister accepts as being equivalent in
    content to the marine advanced first aid course.

<!-- -->

- **[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a vessel shall provide to the master
  written instructions that, at a minimum, determine the procedures and,
  if applicable, the policies, within the meaning of those terms in
  section A-I/14 of the STCW Code, to be followed to ensure that each
  member of the complement, before being assigned any duty,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} becomes familiar with

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the shipboard equipment that are specific to the
      vessel,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the operational instructions that are specific
      to the vessel, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} their assigned duties; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} can effectively perform their assigned duties when
    performing duties vital to safety or the prevention or mitigation of
    pollution.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master shall ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the procedures and, if
    applicable, the policies referred to in subsection (1), each member
    of the vessel's crew, at the beginning of their employment, is
    trained on the subject-matter referred to in paragraph (1)(a) and
    can effectively perform their assigned duties when performing duties
    vital to safety or the prevention or mitigation of pollution, and
    that thereafter the member's knowledge is maintained up to date; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a record of training on the subject-matter and
    training referred to in subsections (1) and 205(1) that includes the
    following information is kept readily available for inspection by a
    marine safety inspector, on board the vessel or, if the vessel does
    not travel more than five nautical miles from its home port, in its
    home port:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the name of each member of the complement who has
      been trained,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the equipment they were trained on,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the subject-matter they were trained on, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the days on which they were trained.

#### [Minimum Complement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730594 .Subheading}

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a vessel shall ensure that the minimum
  complement of the vessel meets the requirements of this section.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person performing the duties of a position listed
  on the Safe Manning Document may be assigned to various duties in
  order to meet the requirements of more than one provision of this
  section.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The minimum complement of a vessel shall be
  sufficient in number to ensure compliance with the requirements set
  out in sections 320 to 322 and shall consist of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the master;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if required by paragraph 212(4)(b), the chief mate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person in charge of the machinery, except if the
    vessel

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a passenger-carrying vessel and has a
      propulsive power of not more than 75 kW,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is not a passenger-carrying vessel and has a
      propulsive power of not more than 750 kW, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} is exempted under section 217 from the
      application of sections 218 to 226;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the persons required to keep

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the deck watch as set out in sections 213 to 216,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the engineering watch as set out in sections 223
      to 225, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the radio watch as set out in sections 266 and
      267;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the [Vessel Fire Safety
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2017-14) require that the vessel
    be provided with a fire patrol, a sufficient number of persons to
    ensure compliance with those Regulations;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is not a fishing vessel and is
    engaged on a voyage of a duration of more than three days that is an
    unlimited voyage or a near coastal voyage, Class 1, a person
    designated to take charge of medical care on board the vessel who is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a physician, if the vessel is carrying 100 or
      more crew members, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} qualified in accordance with paragraph 205(8)(b)
      if the vessel is carrying less than 100 crew members;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a person designated to provide first aid on board
    the vessel, that person being qualified in accordance with
    subsection 205(9);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} for each fast rescue boat on board the vessel, two
    teams of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} two persons holding a Proficiency in Fast Rescue
      Boats certificate or endorsement, if the vessel is engaged on a
      near coastal voyage, Class 2 or a sheltered waters voyage, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} three persons holding a Proficiency in Fast
      Rescue Boats certificate or endorsement, if the vessel is engaged
      on an unlimited voyage or a near coastal voyage, Class 1; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any additional persons who may be required on board
    by the ordinary practice of seamen for normal safe operation of the
    vessel, including docking, anchoring and fuelling.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The minimum complement of a vessel, in order to deal
  with an emergency situation, shall consist of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a master;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the persons required to keep

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the deck watch as set out in sections 214 to 216,
      but the additional person on board a vessel of less than 300 gross
      tonnage and the second additional person on board a vessel of less
      than 3 000 gross tonnage may also be assigned to other duties,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the engineering watch as set out in sections 224
      and 225, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the radio watch as set out in section 266;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the principal communicator as set out in section
    267; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the persons needed to simultaneously carry out the
    following tasks:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} operate and use the fire extinguishing equipment
      and systems required by or approved under the [Vessel Fire Safety
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2017-14) to fight a fire at any
      one location on the vessel,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} prepare for launching the survival craft carried
      in accordance with the [Vessel Construction and Equipment
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257) or the [Life Saving
      Equipment Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1436), as the
      case may be,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} operate the vessel's pumping and emergency
      power system,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} direct and control the passengers who are on
      board, and

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} provide communication between the person in
      immediate charge of the vessel and the persons directing and
      controlling the passengers.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The minimum complement of a vessel shall consist of a
  sufficient number of persons to carry out an evacuation and, in the
  case of a passenger-carrying vessel, to implement the evacuation plan
  required by the [Life Saving Equipment
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1436).

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (7), the minimum complement of
  a vessel, in order to deal with a post-abandonment situation, shall
  consist of a sufficient number of certificated persons to meet the
  requirements of sections 208 to 210.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} In order to deal with an evacuation situation or a
  post-abandonment situation, the master may, despite subsection 209(2),
  assign one team for each fast rescue boat carried on board instead of
  the two teams required by that subsection.

- **[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel shall assign, for each lifeboat required to be on
  board by the [Vessel Construction and Equipment
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257) or the [Life Saving
  Equipment Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1436), as the case
  may be,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the lifeboat has a specified capacity of 50 or
    fewer persons, at least two certificated persons; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the lifeboat has a specified capacity of more
    than 50 persons, at least three certificated persons.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a motorized lifeboat, the master shall
  ensure that one of the persons assigned to it has received the
  necessary training to operate the motor and carry out minor
  adjustments to it.

- **[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel shall assign for each emergency boat or rescue boat
  carried on board, other than a fast rescue boat,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one team of at least two certificated persons, if
    the vessel is engaged on a near coastal voyage, Class 2 or a
    sheltered waters voyage; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one team of at least three certificated persons, if
    the vessel is engaged on an unlimited voyage or a near coastal
    voyage, Class 1.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master of a vessel shall assign for each fast
  rescue boat

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} two teams of at least two persons holding a
    Proficiency in Fast Rescue Boats certificate or endorsement, if the
    vessel is engaged on a near coastal voyage, Class 2 or a sheltered
    waters voyage; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} two teams of at least three persons holding a
    Proficiency in Fast Rescue Boats certificate or endorsement, if the
    vessel is engaged on an unlimited voyage or a near coastal voyage,
    Class 1.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the aggregate capacity of survival craft required
  to accommodate all persons on board a passenger-carrying vessel can be
  met without the need to use one or more emergency boats or rescue
  boats that are on board, the certificated persons required for those
  supplementary emergency boats or rescue boats may be the same persons
  assigned to the lifeboats, life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms
  under sections 208 and 210.

<!-- -->

- **[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  the master of a vessel shall assign

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is a passenger-carrying vessel and is
    engaged on a sheltered waters voyage,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at least one certificated person per two
      inflatable life rafts or inflatable rescue platforms with a
      specified capacity of 25 or fewer persons that are required to be
      carried on board by the [Vessel Construction and Equipment
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257) or the [Life Saving
      Equipment Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1436), as the
      case may be, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} at least one certificated person per inflatable
      life raft or inflatable rescue platform with a specified capacity
      of more than 25 persons that is required to be carried on board by
      the [Vessel Construction and Equipment
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257) or the [Life Saving
      Equipment Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1436), as the
      case may be; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in all other cases, at least one certificated
    person per inflatable life raft or inflatable rescue platform that
    is required to be carried on board by the [Vessel Construction and
    Equipment Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257) or the [Life
    Saving Equipment Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1436), as
    the case may be.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a vessel is engaged on a near coastal voyage,
  Class 2 or a sheltered waters voyage, a certificated person required
  under subsection (1) may be replaced by any member of the complement
  if at least 75% of the complement consists of certificated persons and
  the remainder of the complement is familiarized with the operation of
  survival craft.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a vessel is engaged on a sheltered waters voyage,
  the certificated person may be replaced by a person who holds a
  training certificate in marine emergency duties that is applicable to
  the vessel as set out in the table to subsection 205(3) or, if
  applicable, one of the training certificates referred to in subsection
  205(4).

#### [Safe Manning Requirements]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730657 .Subheading}

**[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** The authorized
representative of a vessel that is not a Safety Convention vessel and
that is required to carry an inspection certificate shall ensure that
the vessel meets the safe manning requirements specified in the Safe
Manning Document established for the vessel by the Minister in
accordance with paragraph 202(3)(b).

#### [Masters and Deck Officers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730659 .Subheading}

- **[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  section applies to a fishing vessel beginning on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} November 7, 2008 if the vessel is of 60 gross
    tonnage or less and more than 15 m in overall length;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} November 7, 2009 if the vessel is more than 14 m
    but not more than 15 m in overall length;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} November 7, 2010 if the vessel is more than 13 m
    but not more than 14 m in overall length;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} November 7, 2012 if the vessel is more than 12 m
    but not more than 13 m in overall length;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} November 7, 2015 if the vessel is more than 6 m but
    not more than 12 m in overall length; or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} November 7, 2016 if the vessel is 6 m or less in
    overall length.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section applies to a vessel of up to 10 gross
  tonnage that is not a fishing vessel or passenger-carrying vessel,
  beginning on November 7, 2010.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section applies to a passenger-carrying vessel
  of up to 5 gross tonnage or not more than 8 m in overall length that
  is not a fishing vessel, beginning on November 7, 2009.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every vessel that is engaged on a voyage shall have
  on board, and its authorized representative shall employ,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (6), a master;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel of at least 500 gross
    tonnage or that carries more than 50 passengers, a chief mate; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a sufficient number of deck officers to satisfy the
    requirements of sections 213 to 216.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every person who holds a certificate set out in
  column 1 of table 1 to this section may perform the duties of a
  position referred to in any of columns 2 to 5 on board a vessel that
  is engaged on a class of voyage set out in the heading of the column
  that applies to that position, subject to any limitations indicated.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} Every person who holds a certificate set out in
  column 1 of table 2 to this section may perform the duties of a
  position referred to in any of columns 2 to 5 on board a vessel
  engaged on a limited, contiguous waters voyage whose only activity is
  in respect of the catch or harvest of another vessel or aquaculture
  facility or a fishing vessel that is engaged on a class of voyage set
  out in the heading to that column, subject to any limitations
  indicated.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} A certificate issued before the day on which this
  section comes into force and renewed under section 105 retains the
  validity that it had under the [Canada Shipping Act](/eng/acts/S-9),
  except in the case of a certificate referred to in column 1 of
  paragraphs 10(e) and 25(b), items 28 and 31 and paragraphs 38(b) and
  39(b) of Schedule 1 to Part 1 which is subject to any limitations set
  out on the renewed certificate.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} The master of a fishing vessel of up to 15 gross
  tonnage or not more than 12 m in overall length who has acquired at
  least 7 fishing seasons, with no two of those seasons occurring in the
  same year, as master of a fishing vessel before the coming into force
  of this section is not required to hold the training certificate
  referred to in column 1 of item 6 of table 2 to this section in order
  to perform the duties set out in columns 4 or 5 of that item.

  - [Note:]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of items 5 and 6, [[near coastal
    voyage, Class 2]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes an inland voyage on
    Lake Superior or Lake Huron that is not a sheltered waters voyage.

#### [Deck Watch]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730695 .Subheading}

**[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 252,
the master of a vessel shall ensure that its intended voyage is planned
and that a deck watch is maintained in accordance with Parts 2, 3 and
3-1 of section A-VIII/2 of the STCW Code.

**[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214 .sectionLabel}** Subject to paragraph
216(2)(b) and subsection 216(4), no person shall act, and no master
shall permit a person to act, as a member of the deck watch of a vessel
unless the person holds a certificate appropriate to the class of
vessel, the area in which the vessel operates and the duties to be
performed by the person.

- **[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel shall be on duty as required by the ordinary
  practice of seamen.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master of a vessel shall not be counted as a
  member of the deck watch unless the vessel is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} securely anchored in port or securely moored to
    shore;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} of not more than 1 000 gross tonnage; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} of more than 1 000 gross tonnage but less than
    3 000 gross tonnage and at least three deck watches are established.

<!-- -->

- **[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  minimum deck watch required by this section shall be supplemented if
  the master determines that it is required by the ordinary practice of
  seamen.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The deck watch shall consist of at least the
  following persons:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person in charge of the deck watch who

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in all cases, holds a certificate of competency
      that authorizes them to perform watchkeeping duties and radio
      watch duties,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is equipped with a VHF
      radiotelephone installation, holds a radio operator certificate
      that is appropriate to the class of vessel and the area in which
      the vessel operates, in accordance with Division 7, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} if an electronic chart display and information
      system (ECDIS) is being used to meet the chart requirements set
      out in section 142 of the [Navigation Safety Regulations,
      2020](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-216),

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} holds a training certificate in ECDIS, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} has been provided by the master with written
        instructions in the operation of, and has been familiarized
        with, the ECDIS and its back-up arrangements carried on board
        the vessel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsections (3) and (4), in the case of
    a vessel of at least 5 gross tonnage, an additional person who
    holds, if the vessel is at least 500 gross tonnage, at a minimum, an
    Able Seafarer certificate or a Bridge Watch Rating certificate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsections (4) and (5), if the vessel
    is of more than 1 000 gross tonnage and is not securely anchored in
    port or securely moored to shore, a second additional person who
    holds, at a minimum, an Able Seafarer certificate or a Bridge Watch
    Rating certificate; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person in charge of the radio watch who is
    qualified in accordance with section 266, unless the person in
    charge of the deck watch is so qualified and is also in charge of
    the radio watch.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} An additional person is not required if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is engaged in a log sorting or yarding
    operation that is carried out at a booming ground and that does not
    use lines or chains;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is of up to 500 gross tonnage,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} affords an unobstructed all-round view from the
      steering position, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} is engaged on a voyage of not more than five
      nautical miles within the limits of a harbour, in good visibility
      between sunrise and sunset;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is a tug assisting another vessel while
    attached to it by a tow line; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is of up to 1 000 gross tonnage and is
    securely anchored in port or securely moored to shore.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The additional person and the second additional
  person assigned to the same deck watch are not both required to hold
  one of the certificates referred to in paragraph (2)(b) or (c) if
  either person, but not both, is a rating under training to obtain one
  of those certificates.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6), a second additional person
  is not required on board a stationary MOU, or on board any other
  vessel of more than 1 000 gross tonnage if that vessel is fitted with
  toilet facilities that are adjacent to the navigating bridge for the
  use of the deck watch, as well as fitted with the equipment listed in
  the schedule to this Part, which equipment shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in good working order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} suitably illuminated for night operation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} used in accordance with the ordinary practice of
    seamen.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A second additional person is required on board a
  vessel that meets the requirements of subsection (5) if the use of the
  automatic steering system is prohibited by local by-laws or its use
  could interfere with prompt helm action in the following
  circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} restricted visibility;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} traffic density; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} hazardous navigational situations.

#### [Engineers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730733 .Subheading}

**[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** Sections 218 to 226 do
not apply in respect of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} vessels of less than 5 gross tonnage;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} vessels of open construction; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} vessels propelled by outboard engines that are not
  permanently attached to the vessel.

**[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218 .sectionLabel}** An Engineer certificate
shall correspond to the vessel's propulsion type as follows:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a motor vessel other than a fishing
  vessel, a certificate of the motor ship category;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a steamship, a certificate of the
  steamship category; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a motor-driven fishing vessel, a
  certificate of the motor ship or motor-driven fishing vessel category.

<!-- -->

- **[[219]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-219 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  passenger-carrying vessel that is engaged on a voyage referred to in
  column 1 of the table to this subsection and that has a propulsive
  power within a range set out in column 2 shall have on board, and its
  authorized representative shall employ, for each certificate referred
  to in column 3 that corresponds to that voyage and range, one person
  who holds, at a minimum, that certificate in the category appropriate
  to the vessel's propulsion type.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A passenger-carrying vessel that has a propulsive
  power of at least 750 kW but not more than 1 499 kW and that is
  engaged on a limited near coastal voyage, Class 2 or a sheltered
  waters voyage, of less than 6 hours' duration, may have on board and
  its authorized representative may employ, a person who holds a Small
  Vessel Machinery Operator certificate, instead of the engineer
  required by paragraph (b) of column 3 of item 4 of the table to
  subsection (1), if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel has a propulsion system comprised of at
    least two independent engines with respect to their control and fuel
    systems and that duplication allows for continued propulsion and
    steering should one engine fail;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the propulsion system is controlled from the bridge
    and has all the necessary gauges, alarms and engine and emergency
    controls;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} continuous radio contact is maintained with the
    home base;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the authorized representative provides

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a list of pre-departure procedures and
      verifications, which procedures and verifications shall be carried
      out by the small vessel machinery operator before each departure,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a maintenance schedule meeting the
      recommendations of the manufacturer of the main engines which
      maintenance shall be carried out by

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} an engineer who holds at least a Fourth-class
        Engineer certificate, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} a service firm accredited by the manufacturer
        of the main engines with which the authorized representative has
        entered into a maintenance contract; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} records of the pre-departure procedures are readily
    available, for inspection by a marine safety inspector, on board the
    vessel or, if the vessel does not travel more than five nautical
    miles from its home port, readily available in its home port.

<!-- -->

- **[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  cargo vessel that is engaged on a voyage set out in column 1 of the
  table to this subsection and has a propulsive power within a range set
  out in column 2 shall have on board, and its authorized representative
  shall employ, for each certificate referred to in column 3 that
  corresponds to that voyage and range, one engineer who holds, at a
  minimum, that certificate in the category appropriate to the vessel's
  propulsion type.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A cargo vessel that has a propulsive power of at
  least 750 kW but not more than 1 999 kW and that is engaged on a
  limited near coastal voyage, Class 2 or a sheltered waters voyage, of
  less than 6 hours' duration, may have on board and its authorized
  representative may employ, a person who holds a Small Vessel Machinery
  Operator certificate, instead of the engineer required by
  paragraph (a) in column 3 of item 5 of the table to subsection (1), if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel has a propulsion system comprised of at
    least two independent engines with respect to their control and fuel
    systems and that duplication allows for continued propulsion and
    steering should one engine fail;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the propulsion system is controlled from the bridge
    and has all the necessary gauges, alarms and engine and emergency
    controls;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} continuous radio contact is maintained with the
    home base;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the authorized representative provides

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a list of pre-departure procedures and
      verifications, which procedures and verifications shall be carried
      out by the small vessel machinery operator before each departure,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a maintenance schedule meeting the
      recommendations of the manufacturer of the main engines, which
      maintenance shall be carried out by

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} an engineer who holds at least a Fourth-class
        Engineer certificate, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} a service firm, accredited by the manufacturer
        of the main engines, with which the authorized representative
        has entered into a maintenance contract; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} records of the pre-departure procedures are readily
    available, for inspection by a marine safety inspector, on board the
    vessel or, if the vessel does not travel more than five nautical
    miles from its home port, readily available in its home port.

<!-- -->

- **[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  tug that is engaged on a voyage set out in column 1 of the table to
  this subsection and that has a propulsive power within a range set out
  in column 2 shall have on board, and its authorized representative
  shall employ, for each certificate referred to in column 3 that
  corresponds to that voyage and range, one engineer who holds, at a
  minimum, that certificate in the category appropriate to that vessel's
  propulsion type.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A tug that has a propulsive power of at least 750 kW
  but not more than 1 999 kW and that is engaged on a near coastal
  voyage, Class 2, or a tug that has a propulsive power of at least
  1 500 kW but not more than 2 999 kW and that is engaged on a limited
  near coastal voyage, Class 2 or a sheltered waters voyage, of less
  than 6 hours' duration, may have on board and its authorized
  representative may employ, a person who holds a Small Vessel Machinery
  Operator certificate, instead of the engineer required by
  paragraph (a) in column 3 of item 3 or 4 of the table to subsection
  (1), if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the tug has a propulsion system comprised of at
    least two independent engines with respect to their control and fuel
    systems and that duplication allows for continued propulsion and
    steering should one engine fail;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the propulsion system is controlled from the bridge
    with all necessary gauges, alarms and engine and emergency controls;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} continuous radio contact is maintained with the
    home base;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the authorized representative provides

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a list of pre-departure procedures and
      verifications, which procedures and verifications shall be carried
      out by the small vessel machinery operator before each departure,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a maintenance schedule meeting the
      recommendations of the manufacturer of the main engines, which
      maintenance shall be carried out by

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} an engineer who holds at least a Fourth-class
        Engineer certificate, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} a service firm accredited by the manufacturer
        of the main engines with which the authorized representative has
        entered into a maintenance contract; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} records of the pre-departure procedures are readily
    available, for inspection by a marine safety inspector, on board the
    vessel or, if the vessel does not travel more than five nautical
    miles from its home port, readily available in its home port.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A harbour tug of less than 500 gross tonnage that is
  not a passenger-carrying vessel, that is being used to assist a vessel
  to dock or undock and that is at no time more than five nautical miles
  from an accessible dock offering refuge may have on board and its
  authorized representative may employ, a person who holds a Small
  Vessel Machinery Operator certificate, instead of the person required
  by paragraph (b) or (c) in column 3 of item 4 of the table to
  subsection (1), if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the harbour tug complies with the requirements
    applicable to periodically unattended machinery spaces under the
    [Vessel Construction and Equipment
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257) or with Schedule VIII to
    the [Marine Machinery Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-90-264), as
    the case may be;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a shore engineer is available and ready to
    intervene should the master determine that the vessel requires
    immediate assistance;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the authorized representative provides a list of
    pre-departure procedures and verifications, which procedures and
    verifications shall be carried out by the small vessel machinery
    operator before each departure;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} records of the pre-departure procedures are readily
    available, for inspection by a marine safety inspector, on board the
    vessel or, if the vessel does not travel more than five nautical
    miles from its home port, readily available in its home port; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} continuous radio contact is maintained with the
    home base.

**[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** Every fishing vessel
that is engaged on a voyage set out in column 1 of the table to this
section and that has a propulsive power within a range set out in column
2 shall have on board, and its authorized representative shall employ,
for each certificate set out in column 3 that corresponds to that voyage
and range, one engineer who holds, at least that certificate appropriate
to the vessel's propulsion type.
