</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">(b) 2 000 to 5 000</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom">(b) Second-class</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">(c) more than 5 000</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom">(c) First-class and Second-class</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td rowspan="3" class="noBorderRight">2</td>
<td rowspan="3" class="noBorderRight" scope="rowgroup">Near coastal
voyage, Class 2 or sheltered waters voyage</td>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">(a) 750 to 2 999</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom">(a) Fourth-class</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom">(b) 3 000 to 5 000</td>
<td class="noBorderBottom">(b) Third-class</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td class="noBorderRight">(c) more than 5 000</td>
<td>(c) Second-class</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><p>TABLE</p>
<p>Engineer Certificates — Fishing Vessels</p></figcaption>
</figure>

#### [Engineering Watch]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730813 .Subheading}

**[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 253,
the chief engineer of a vessel shall ensure, in consultation with the
master, that its voyage is planned and that an engineering watch is
maintained in accordance with Parts 2, 3, and 3-2 of section A-VIII/2 of
the STCW Code.

- **[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 226, the chief
  engineer of a vessel shall ensure, in consultation with the master,
  that the engineering watch of the vessel consists of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel referred to in subsection
    (2), a person in charge of the engineering watch who, at a minimum,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} holds a certificate that complies with section
      218 and is required by a table to subsection 219(1), 220(1) or
      221(1) or the table to section 222,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} holds

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a passenger-carrying vessel, a
        cargo vessel or a tug, a Fourth-class Engineer certificate,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a motor-driven fishing vessel
        that has a propulsive power of not more than 2 000 kW, a
        Watchkeeping Engineer, Motor-driven Fishing Vessel certificate,
        or

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a fishing vessel that has a
        propulsive power of more than 2 000 kW, a Fourth-class Engineer
        certificate; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel that has a propulsive power
    of more than 750 kW, an additional person who holds, at a minimum,
    an Engine-room Rating certificate.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(a) applies if the vessel is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a passenger-carrying vessel that has a propulsive
    power of more than 75 kW;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a fishing or cargo vessel that has a propulsive
    power of more than 750 kW;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a tug that has a propulsive power of more than 750
    kW and is engaged on a voyage other than a limited near coastal
    voyage, Class 2 or a sheltered waters voyage; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a tug that has a propulsive power of 1 500 kW or
    more.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(b) does not apply if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the machinery essential to the safe operation of
    the vessel has automatic operational features that, while the
    machinery is in operation, provide fuel to the machinery and
    lubricate it from a supply of lubricant that is sufficient to enable
    the machinery to operate continuously at full load for a period of
    at least 24 hours; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the propulsion system of the vessel is remotely
    controlled from the bridge or the vessel is not manoeuvring.

<!-- -->

- **[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  vessel that is equipped in accordance with the requirements applicable
  to periodically unattended machinery spaces under the [Vessel
  Construction and Equipment Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257)
  or with Schedule VIII to the [Marine Machinery
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-90-264) may operate with
  periodically unattended machinery spaces if the remote control and
  monitoring systems in those spaces are inspected at intervals not
  exceeding 12 months and the inspection certificate contains a notation
  confirming the inspection.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a vessel of at least
  500 gross tonnage when it is manoeuvring or proceeding in a narrow
  channel or fairway.

#### [Dual Capacity]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730835 .Subheading}

- **[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall act in the dual capacity of master and engineer on board
  a motor vessel that is more than 20 m in length.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person may act in the dual capacity of master and
  engineer on board a motor vessel that is 20 m or less in length if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person holds a certificate enabling them to act
    in the capacity of master on board that vessel and, if these
    Regulations require that an Engineer certificate be held, also holds
    that Engineer certificate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's engine is installed so that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} it can be controlled from the steering station,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a person at the steering station may readily
      detect any defects in the engine and make any necessary
      adjustments to the engine while keeping a navigational lookout;
      and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} there is on board, in addition to the person acting
    in the dual capacity, at least one crew member who is at least 18
    years of age and able to render any assistance that might be
    necessary in an emergency.

#### [Cooks]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730844 .Subheading}

**[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** If a vessel has on
board and employs a cook, the authorized representative shall ensure
that the cook holds a Ship's Cook certificate if the vessel

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} has a minimum complement of 10 or more members;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} is used in the transportation of cargo or passengers
  for the purpose of trade; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} is engaged on a international voyage other than a
  voyage to the United States or Saint Pierre and Miquelon.

#### [Endorsements and Certificates --- Tankers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730849 .Subheading}

- **[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  master, chief mate, chief engineer or second engineer employed on
  board an oil tanker, chemical tanker or liquefied gas tanker shall
  hold a Specialized Oil Tanker Training endorsement, Specialized
  Chemical Tanker Training endorsement or Specialized Liquefied Gas
  Tanker Training endorsement, as the case may be.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An oil tanker, a chemical tanker, a liquefied gas
  tanker or other vessel carrying oil, liquefied gas or chemicals as
  cargo shall have on board, and its authorized representative shall
  employ, for each duty set out in column 1 of the table to this
  subsection, one person who holds the certificate or endorsement set
  out in column 2.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the tanker uses an inert gas system or a crude oil
  washing system,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the persons who have responsibilities in respect of
    the operation of those systems shall

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} hold a training certificate indicating that they
      have successfully completed training on inert gas systems or crude
      oil washing systems or both depending on which systems the tanker
      is equipped with, or have at least one year's experience on
      tankers performing duties that include the discharge of cargo and
      associated inert gas system or crude oil washing operations,
      depending on which systems the tanker is equipped with, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an oil tanker, have participated
      in at least two crude oil washing operations, one of which was on
      board the oil tanker on which they are required to undertake the
      responsibility of cargo discharge, or on board a similarly
      equipped oil tanker; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the tanker is an oil tanker, the other persons
    assigned tasks referred to in the vessel's operations and equipment
    manual, which manual shall comply with Regulation 13B(5) of Annex I
    to the Pollution Convention, shall have

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} acquired at least 6 months of experience on one
      or more tankers where they have been involved in cargo discharge
      operations, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} received on-board training in the operation of
      the tankers' inert gas system and crude oil washing system.

#### [Endorsements and Certificates --- Passenger-carrying Vessels]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730867 .Subheading}

- **[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), in the
  case of a ro-ro vessel that carries more than 12 passengers, is more
  than 500 gross tonnage and is engaged on a voyage other than a
  sheltered waters voyage, every master, chief mate, chief engineer,
  second engineer and, if assigned one of the following duties, other
  persons employed on that vessel shall hold a Specialized Passenger
  Safety Management (Ro-Ro Vessels) certificate or endorsement:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} loading, discharging or securing cargo;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} closing hull openings;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensuring passenger safety in emergency situations;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} assisting in embarking or disembarking passengers.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), every person, other than
  the persons referred to in subsection (1), employed on board a ro-ro
  vessel of more than 500 gross tonnage that carries more than 12
  passengers and that is engaged on a voyage other than a sheltered
  waters voyage shall hold a Passenger Safety Management certificate or
  endorsement if their assigned duties include any of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} assisting passengers in emergency situations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} providing direct service to passengers in passenger
    spaces; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} assisting in embarking or disembarking passengers.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) and (2) apply in respect of a vessel
  that is not a Safety Convention vessel, beginning on November 7, 2011.

<!-- -->

- **[[230]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-230
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), each
  master, officer and other member of the complement assigned specific
  duties on the muster list of a vessel of more than 500 gross tonnage
  that carries more than 12 passengers, that is engaged on a voyage
  other than a sheltered waters voyage and that is not a ro-ro vessel,
  shall hold a Passenger Safety Management certificate or endorsement if
  their assigned duties include any of the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} assisting passengers in emergency situations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} providing direct service to passengers in passenger
    spaces; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} assisting in embarking or disembarking passengers.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies in respect of a vessel that is
  not a Safety Convention vessel, beginning on November 7, 2011.

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Mobile Offshore Units]{.HTitleText2} {#h-730885 .Subheading}

#### [Training and Familiarization]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730886 .Subheading}

- **[[231]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-231
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  offshore installation manager shall ensure that visitors and personnel
  not directly employed on board the MOU or regularly assigned on board
  the MOU, who are on board for a short period and who have no tasks in
  relation to the normal operations of the unit shall receive offshore
  orientation or familiarization training, as well as instruction in
  personal survival techniques and workplace safety that ensure that
  personnel know of the MOU's organizational structure and chain of
  command and are able to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} communicate with other persons on board regarding
    basic safety matters and understand safety information symbols,
    signs and alarm signals, especially with respect to knowing what to
    do if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a person falls overboard,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} fire, smoke or hydrogen sulphide is detected, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the fire, abandon ship or toxic gas alarm or
      other general alarm is sounded;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} locate and don a lifejacket and, if provided on
    board, an immersion suit;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} identify muster and embarkation stations and
    emergency escape routes;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} raise the alarm and properly use a portable fire
    extinguisher;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} take immediate action on encountering an accident
    or a medical emergency on board;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} close and open the fire, weathertight and
    watertight doors fitted on the MOU, other than those for hull
    openings; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} follow the MOU's safe work practices in accordance
    with the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) and respect the
    permit-to-work system applicable on board the MOU.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The orientation or training as well as the
  instruction referred to in paragraphs (1)(d) to (g) may be reduced in
  scope or omitted in the case of persons not staying on board the MOU
  overnight, but only if those persons are accompanied while on board by
  a person referred to in section 232 or 233.

<!-- -->

- **[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  offshore installation manager shall ensure that personnel without
  designated responsibility for the safety and survival of others
  receive

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the orientation or training as well as the
    instructions set out in section 231;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} hydrogen sulphide training, if there is a
    possibility that substance will be found on the MOU;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} training on workplace hazardous materials
    information systems (WHMIS); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} before acquiring 1 month of service on board the
    MOU, basic training in

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} proficiency in personal survival techniques,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} fire prevention and fire fighting,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} personal safety,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} familiarization and orientation on the general
      arrangement of the MOU,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} operating systems of the MOU,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} equipment and procedures on board the MOU,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} the organizational structure on board the MOU,

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} safety philosophy and contingency plans on
      board the MOU,

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} alarm procedures for emergency situations on
      board the MOU, and

    - [(x)]{.lawlabel} the critical need to bring any abnormal situation
      on board the MOU to the attention of a responsible person.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The training referred to in paragraph (1)(d) shall be
  administered by a crew member having the pertinent specialized
  training set out in section 233.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of an MOU shall ensure
  that a record of training is kept, and is presented on request for
  inspection by a marine safety inspector, to allow the inspector to
  ensure that all personnel without designated responsibility for the
  safety and survival of others have achieved the required standard of
  competence within the five preceding years.

<!-- -->

- **[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  offshore installation manager shall ensure that a crew member who is
  assigned specific duties on the muster list holds

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the crew member is in charge of a survival
    craft, a training certificate in marine emergency duties with
    respect to proficiency in survival craft and rescue boats other than
    fast rescue boats;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the crew member is a member of a fire fighting
    team, a training certificate with respect to marine emergency duties
    in advanced fire fighting;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the crew member is assigned to operate a fast
    rescue boat, a training certificate with respect to proficiency in
    fast rescue boats; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the crew member is designated to provide first
    aid, a marine advanced first aid certificate.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Instead of holding a training certificate required by
  subsection (1), a crew member may hold a certificate referred to in
  the Canadian East Coast Offshore Petroleum Industry, Standard Practice
  for the Training and Qualification of Personnel, published by the
  Canadian Association of Petroleum Producers, if the Minister has
  determined it to be equivalent to the certificate required by
  subsection (1).

#### [Minimum Complement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730925 .Subheading}

- **[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of an MOU shall ensure that its minimum
  complement consists of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offshore installation manager who holds an
    Offshore Installation Manager certificate appropriate to the type of
    MOU;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a barge supervisor who holds a Barge Supervisor
    certificate appropriate to the type of MOU;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an MOU/surface, a ballast control
    operator who holds a Ballast Control Operator certificate and who
    may be, if the ballast controls are located within the bridge or the
    space in which the deck watch is conducted, the person in charge of
    the deck watch;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an MOU/self-propelled, the number of
    persons necessary to ensure a continuous deck watch, each of whom
    shall hold, at a minimum, a Watchkeeping Mate certificate;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a stationary MOU, the number of
    persons necessary to ensure a continuous deck watch;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a person in charge of the machinery who holds a
    Maintenance Supervisor certificate appropriate to the type of MOU;
    and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} an engineer in charge of the engineering watch who
    holds, at a minimum, a Fourth-class Engineer, Motor Ship certificate
    or a Maintenance Supervisor certificate appropriate to the type of
    MOU.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of an MOU/surface shall
  ensure that the MOU has a sufficient number of certified ballast
  control operators to ensure that the ballast control position is
  continuously crewed by a certified ballast control operator.

**[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235 .sectionLabel}** Every MOU shall have on
board and its authorized representative shall employ

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} for each fast rescue boat on board, at least one team
  of three persons, each of whom hold a training certificate with
  respect to proficiency in fast rescue boats;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} for each rescue boat on board other than a rescue
  boat referred to in paragraph (a), at least one team of three persons,
  each of whom holds a training certificate in marine emergency duties
  with respect to proficiency in survival craft and rescue boats other
  than fast rescue boats; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} for each of the lifeboats that are required for the
  evacuation of all persons on board, two persons, each of whom holds a
  training certificate in marine emergency duties with respect to
  proficiency in survival craft and rescue boats other than fast rescue
  boats.

#### [Safe Manning Document]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730940 .Subheading}

- **[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of an MOU shall apply to the Minister for
  and the Minister shall issue, following that application, a document
  that specifies:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the minimum number of members of the complement as
    established in accordance with section 234;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the certificates to be held by the members of the
    complement in accordance with sections 177 to 183; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, any conditions or restrictions
    referred to on the certificates referred to in paragraph (b).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of the MOU shall ensure
  that the document referred to in subsection (1) is carried on board.

#### [Deck Watch]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730947 .Subheading}

**[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** The offshore
installation manager of an MOU shall ensure that a deck watch is
maintained in accordance with Parts 3 and 3-1 of section A-VIII/2 of the
STCW Code.

- **[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  minimum deck watch required by this section shall be supplemented as
  required by the ordinary practice of seamen for normal safe operation
  of the MOU.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The offshore installation manager may be counted as a
  member of the deck watch.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The offshore installation manager of an MOU shall
  ensure that each deck watch on the MOU consists of at least the
  following persons:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person in charge of the deck watch who

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in all cases, holds a certificate demonstrating
      that they have the necessary knowledge or have received
      instructions sufficient to allow them to be capable of properly
      operating the navigational and radio equipment with which the MOU
      is fitted, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an MOU/self-propelled, holds, at
      a minimum, a Watchkeeping Mate certificate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an additional person who, in the case of an
    MOU/self-propelled of at least 500 gross tonnage, holds, at a
    minimum, an Able Seafarer certificate or a Bridge Watch Rating
    certificate; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person in charge of the radio watch who is
    qualified in accordance with section 266, unless the person in
    charge of the deck watch is so qualified.

#### [Engineering Watch]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730958 .Subheading}

**[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239 .sectionLabel}** The offshore
installation manager of an MOU shall ensure that the engineering watch
on board is maintained in accordance with Parts 3 and 3-2 of section
A-VIII/2 of the STCW Code.

- **[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  offshore installation manager of an MOU shall ensure that the
  engineering watch consists of an engineer in charge of the engineering
  watch who holds, at a minimum, a Fourth-class Engineer, Motor Ship
  certificate or a Maintenance Supervisor certificate appropriate to
  that MOU's type.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An MOU that meets the requirements applicable to
  periodically unattended machinery spaces under the [Vessel
  Construction and Equipment Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257)
  or of Schedule VIII to the [Marine Machinery
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-90-264) may operate with
  periodically unattended machinery spaces if the remote control and
  monitoring systems in those spaces are inspected at intervals not
  exceeding 12 months and the inspection certificate contains a notation
  confirming the inspection.

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Foreign Vessels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-730963 .Subheading}

#### [Minimum Complement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730964 .Subheading}

**[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** The authorized
representative of a vessel shall ensure that its complement consists of
at least the following persons:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the master;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the persons needed to form the

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} life-saving team,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} radio watch,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} deck watch, and

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} engineering watch;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person in charge of the vessel's machinery unless
  the vessel is a cargo vessel, a tug or a fishing vessel with a
  propulsive power of not more than 750 kW; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if applicable, the persons required by the Safe
  Manning Document or equivalent document required by subsection 242(2)
  or (3).

#### [Safe Manning Document or Equivalent Document]{.HTitleText3} {#h-730974 .Subheading}

- **[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  section does not apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} fishing vessels;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} wooden ships of primitive build referred to in
    paragraph A of regulation 3 of Chapter I of SOLAS;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} cargo vessels and tugs of less than 500 gross
    tonnage; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} vessels that are engaged on a near coastal voyage,
    Class 2 or a sheltered waters voyage.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a Safety Convention
  vessel shall ensure that the vessel carries on board a Safe Manning
  Document or an equivalent document issued by the Administration as
  evidence that the vessel has on board the minimum complement necessary
  to satisfy the requirements of IMO Resolution A.890(21), Principles of
  Safe Manning, or any other resolution that replaces it.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel that is not
  a Safety Convention vessel shall ensure that it carries on board a
  document issued by the Administration and containing, in English,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the following identification information about the
    vessel:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} its name,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} its port of registry,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} its official number,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the type of vessel,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} its gross registered tonnage, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} its propulsive power and propulsion type;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a table showing

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the minimum number of members of the complement,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the certificates to be held by the members of
      the complement, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any conditions or restrictions referred to in
      the certificates referred to in subparagraph (ii);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a statement by the Administration that the vessel
    named in the document is safely crewed when it carries at least the
    complement referred to in the document, whose members hold the
    certificates indicated in that document, subject to any conditions
    or restrictions referred to on those certificates;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a statement of any conditions or restrictions on
    the validity of the document by reference to particulars of the
    vessel and the nature of the service in which the vessel is
    employed; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the date of issue and the expiry date, if any, of
    the document together with a signature on behalf of, and the seal
    of, the Administration.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel engaged in
  the coasting trade shall ensure that there is on board an endorsement
  issued by the Minister that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contains a statement that the document, issued by
    the Administration, meets the requirements of Division 2; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} sets out the requirements that the vessel shall
    meet to comply with the crewing standards referred to in Division 2.

#### [Issuance and Validity of Certificates]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731000 .Subheading}

- **[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every master, mate, engineer or any
  other person who forms part of an engineering watch or deck watch on
  board a vessel shall hold a certificate of competency that is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} issued by the Administration;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} endorsed by the Administration to attest to its
    recognition; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} accompanied by an attestation from the
    Administration that an application for an endorsement has been made
    under section I/10 of the STCW Convention.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The certificate referred to in subsection (1) shall
  be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} appropriate to the duties performed by that person
    on the vessel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} endorsed by the Administration as meeting the
    provisions of the STCW Convention; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} valid under the STCW Convention for the vessel and
    the voyage on which the vessel is engaged.

#### [Deck Watch]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731010 .Subheading}

**[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 252,
the master of a vessel shall ensure that a voyage is planned and a deck
watch is maintained in accordance with Parts 2, 3 and 3-1 of section
A-VIII/2 of the STCW Code.

**[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245 .sectionLabel}** Subject to subsection
247(2), no person shall act, and no master shall permit a person to act,
as a member of the deck watch unless the person holds a certificate
appropriate to the class of vessel and the voyages on which the vessel
is engaged.

**[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246 .sectionLabel}** The master of a vessel
shall be on duty as required by the ordinary practice of seamen.

- **[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel shall ensure that the deck watch consists, at a
  minimum of,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person in charge of the deck watch who holds a
    certificate authorizing them to be the officer in charge of the deck
    watch;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (2), an additional person
    who, in the case of a vessel that is at least 500 gross tonnage
    holds, at a minimum, a Bridge Watch Rating certificate or an
    equivalent to the Able Seafarer certificate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsections (2) and (3), a second
    additional person who, in the case of a vessel of more than 1 000
    gross tonnage holds, at a minimum, a Bridge Watch Rating certificate
    or an equivalent to the Able Seafarer certificate; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a person in charge of the radio watch who has
    qualifications equivalent to those set out in section 266, unless
    the person in charge of the deck watch is so qualified and is also
    in charge of the radio watch.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The additional person and the second additional
  person assigned to the same deck watch are not both required to hold
  one of the certificates referred to in paragraph (1)(b) or (c) if
  either person, but not both, is a rating under training to obtain one
  of those certificates.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), a second additional person
  is not required on board a stationary MOU, or on board any other
  vessel of more than 1 000 gross tonnage if that vessel is fitted with
  toilet facilities that are adjacent to the navigating bridge for the
  use of the deck watch, as well as fitted with the equipment listed in
  the schedule to this Part, which equipment shall be

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in good working order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} suitably illuminated for night operation; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} used in accordance with the ordinary practice of
    seamen.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A second additional person is required on board a
  vessel that meets the requirements of subsection (3) if the use of the
  automatic steering system is prohibited by local by-laws or its use
  could interfere with prompt helm action in the following
  circumstances:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} restricted visibility;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} traffic density; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} hazardous navigational situations.

#### [Engineering Watch]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731029 .Subheading}

**[[248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 253,
the chief engineer of a vessel shall ensure, in consultation with the
master, that its voyage is planned and that an engineering watch is
maintained in accordance with Parts 2, 3 and 3-2 of section A-VIII/2 of
the STCW Code.

- **[[249]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  chief engineer of a vessel shall ensure, in consultation with the
  master, that the engineering watch of the vessel consists of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel that has a propulsive power
    of more than 750 kW, a person in charge of the engineering watch who
    holds, at a minimum, an Engineer certificate appropriate to the
    vessel's propulsion type; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an engine-room rating who holds, at a minimum, the
    equivalent to an Engine-room Rating certificate.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (1)(b) does not apply if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the machinery essential to the safe operation of
    the vessel has automatic operational features that, while the
    machinery is in operation, provide fuel to the machinery and
    lubricate it from a supply of lubricant that is sufficient to enable
    the machinery to operate continuously at full load for a period of
    at least 24 hours; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the propulsion system of the vessel is remotely
    controlled from the bridge or the vessel is not manoeuvring.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 250, a vessel that is equipped in
  accordance with the Administration's requirements for periodically
  unattended machinery spaces may operate with periodically unattended
  machinery spaces if the remote control and monitoring systems for
  those spaces are inspected in accordance with the Administration's
  rules and the inspection certificate contains a notation confirming
  the inspection.

**[[250]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250 .sectionLabel}** At all times that the
vessel is manoeuvring or proceeding in a narrow channel or fairway, the
engineer on watch shall remain present in the engine room unless the
engine room is not suitable for a person to physically stand a watch
during normal operations by reason of the dimensions or configuration of
the room.

#### [Endorsements and Certificates --- Tankers and Passenger-carrying Vessels]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731040 .Subheading}

**[[251]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251 .sectionLabel}** The requirements under
chapter V of the STCW Convention with respect to certificates and
endorsements required for passenger-carrying vessels and tankers apply
to all employees on those vessels who are assigned to the duties
referred to in that chapter.

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Vessels Securely Anchored in Port or Securely Moored to Shore]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731042 .Subheading}

#### [Deck Watch]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731043 .Subheading}

**[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252 .sectionLabel}** The master of a vessel
that is securely anchored in port or securely moored to shore shall
ensure that a deck watch is maintained in accordance with Parts 4, 4-1,
4-3 and 4-5 of section A-VIII/2 of the STCW Code unless the master has
made sufficient alternative arrangements to secure the safety of life
and the protection of the environment, taking into account those Parts.

#### [Engineering Watch]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731045 .Subheading}

- **[[253]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  chief engineer of a vessel that is securely anchored in port or
  securely moored to shore shall ensure, in consultation with the
  master, that an engineering watch is maintained in accordance with
  Parts 4, 4-2, 4-4 and 4-5 of section A-VIII/2 of the STCW Code.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a vessel
  that does not normally maintain an engineering watch in port if the
  chief engineer makes, in consultation with the master, sufficient and
  efficient alternative arrangements to secure the safety of life and
  the protection of the environment, taking into account Parts 4, 4-2,
  4-4 and 4-5 of section A-VIII/2 of the STCW Code.

#### [Radio Watch]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731049 .Subheading}

**[[254]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254 .sectionLabel}** The master of a vessel
that is securely anchored, moored or made fast to the shore or secured
to the bottom shall ensure, in accordance with section 252 of the
[Navigation Safety Regulations, 2020](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-216),
that a continuous listening watch is maintained if, in the master's
opinion, the vessel is in a place where its presence may constitute a
hazard to passing vessels.

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Canadian Special Design Crafts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731051 .Subheading}

#### [Training]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731052 .Subheading}

**[[255]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-255 .sectionLabel}** In addition to
complying with the requirements of section 205, the master shall ensure
that all crew members on board a high-speed craft possess a training
testimonial indicating that they have received the type rating training
specified in sections 18.3.3.6 to 18.3.3.12 of the HSC Code applicable
to the type of vessel and the route that the vessel follows.

#### [Masters and Mates]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731054 .Subheading}

- **[[256]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-256 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  air cushion vessel that is at least 1 000 kg all up weight shall have
  on board, and its authorized representative shall employ, a master
  who, at a minimum, holds

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Master, Limited certificate if the vessel is less
    than 10 000 kg all up weight;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a Master 150 Gross Tonnage, Domestic certificate if
    the vessel is of 10 000 kg or more but less than 50 000 kg all up
    weight;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a Master 500 Gross Tonnage, Domestic certificate if
    the vessel is of 50 000 kg or more but less than 100 000 kg all up
    weight;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a Master 3 000 Gross Tonnage, Domestic certificate
    if the vessel is of 100 000 kg or more all up weight; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} despite paragraphs (b) to (d), a Master, Limited
    certificate if the vessel is engaged on a sheltered waters voyage.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An air cushion vessel that is at least 40 000 kg all
  up weight or that carries more than 50 persons shall have on board,
  and its authorized representative shall employ, a chief mate who, at a
  minimum, holds

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a Chief Mate, Limited certificate if the vessel is
    engaged on a sheltered waters voyage; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a Chief Mate, 150 Gross Tonnage, Domestic
    certificate in all other cases.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The master and the chief mate employed on board an
  air cushion vessel of more than 1 000 kg all up weight shall also hold
  an Air Cushion Vehicle (ACV) Type Rating certificate applicable to the
  type of vessel and the route that the vessel follows.

**[[257]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257 .sectionLabel}** A wing-in-ground craft
shall have on board, and its authorized representative shall employ, a
master who, at a minimum, holds a Master, Limited certificate.

- **[[258]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a high-speed craft, other than an air cushion vessel, and
  any officer having an operational role on the high-speed craft who may
  be called on to operate the craft, shall hold, in addition to the
  certificates and endorsements required by Division 2, a High-Speed
  Craft (HSC) Type Rating certificate applicable to the type of vessel
  and the route that the vessel follows.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a
  high-speed craft engaged on a near coastal voyage, Class 2, or a
  sheltered waters voyage, that has a maximum operating speed of 25
  knots or less.

#### [Engineers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731070 .Subheading}

- **[[259]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-259 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of an air cushion vessel of more than
  1 000 kg but less than 10 000 kg all up weight that is not a
  passenger-carrying vessel or that is certified to carry 50 or fewer
  passengers shall employ for that air cushion vessel an engineer who
  holds, at a minimum, an Air Cushion Vehicle (ACV) Engineer, Class II
  certificate.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master shall ensure that the engineer referred to
  in subsection (1) is on board the air cushion vessel in the case where
  it is engaged on a voyage during which it is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} more than 5 nautical miles from shore or more than
    15 nautical miles from a place of refuge if the vessel is a
    passenger-carrying vessel; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} more than 15 nautical miles from a place of refuge
    if the vessel is not a passenger-carrying vessel.

<!-- -->

- **[[260]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-260 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of an air cushion vessel of an all up weight
  of at least 10 000 kg or of an air cushion vessel that is certified to
  carry more than 50 passengers shall employ for that air cushion vessel
  an engineer who holds an Air Cushion Vehicle (ACV) Engineer, Class I
  certificate.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master shall ensure that the engineer referred to
  in subsection (1) is on board the air cushion vessel in the case where
  it is engaged on a voyage during which it is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} more than 5 nautical miles from shore or more than
    15 nautical miles from a place of refuge if the vessel is a
    passenger-carrying vessel; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} more than 20 nautical miles from a place of refuge
    if the vessel is not a passenger-carrying vessel.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the master of an air cushion vessel is not
  required to have on board that vessel an engineer who holds a
  certificate of competency, the authorized representative shall prepare
  written instructions that set out the pre-departure procedures for
  verifications, inspection and maintenance. The details of the
  procedures and the frequency at which they shall be completed shall be
  in accordance with the manufacturer's recommendations or a manual
  reviewed by a marine safety inspector that is based on the operational
  history of the air cushion vessel.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The master of an air cushion vessel shall ensure that
  the engineer referred to in subsection (1) or 259(1), as the case may
  be, completes the procedures referred to in subsection (3) and that
  records of those completed procedures are maintained for the duration
  of the air cushion vessel's period of service.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of an air cushion
  vessel shall ensure that an engineer employed by the authorized
  representative holds a type rating certificate applicable to the type
  of vessel.

**[[261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-261 .sectionLabel}** The engineers employed
on board a high-speed craft other than an air cushion vessel shall hold
the certificates and endorsements required by Division 2 and, if they
may be called on to operate the high-speed craft, a High-Speed Craft
(HSC) Type Rating certificate.

#### [Sailing Vessels]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731085 .Subheading}

- **[[262]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-262
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  master and chief mate of any sail training vessel or any other sailing
  vessel, of at least 60 gross tonnage or of at least 24 m in length,
  that is a passenger-carrying vessel shall hold a Fore and Aft Sailing
  Vessel, Unlimited endorsement or a Square Rig Sailing Vessel,
  Unlimited endorsement, depending on the vessel's type of rig.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master and chief mate of a vessel referred to in
  subsection (1) may hold, instead of the endorsements referred to in
  that subsection, a Fore and Aft Sailing Vessel, Seasonal (April 15 to
  November 1) endorsement or a Square Rig Sailing Vessel, Seasonal
  (April 15 to November 1) endorsement, depending on the vessel's type
  of rig, if the vessel is used solely for seasonal operation from April
  15 to November 1 of each year and does not engage on unlimited voyages
  or near coastal voyages, Class 1.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section applies beginning on November 7, 2011.

#### [Passenger Submersible Craft]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731090 .Subheading}

- **[[263]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a passenger submersible craft shall hold a certificate
  listed in table 1 to section 212 with a Passenger Submersible Craft
  endorsement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A passenger submersible craft equipped with an
  internal combustion engine of 750 kW or less or an electric propulsion
  system of at least 75 kW but not more than 750 kW shall have on board,
  and its authorized representative shall employ, an engineer who holds,
  at a minimum, a Small Vessel Machinery Operator certificate with a
  Passenger Submersible Craft Endorsement.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} A passenger submersible craft that has a propulsive
  power of more than 750 kW shall have on board, and its authorized
  representative shall employ, the engineers required by section 219,
  who shall also hold a Passenger Submersible craft endorsement.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} If a passenger submersible craft that is a
  passenger-carrying vessel is not required to have on board an engineer
  with a certificate of competency, the authorized representative shall
  assign the maintenance of the vessel's machinery to a technician
  accredited for this purpose by the manufacturer.

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[Radio Watch]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731096 .Subheading}

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731097 .Subheading}

**[[264]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-264 .sectionLabel}** The master of a Safety
Convention vessel shall ensure that it is in compliance with regulation
12 of Chapter IV of SOLAS in respect of radio watches.

**[[265]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-265 .sectionLabel}** The master of a vessel
shall ensure that it is in compliance with

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} paragraphs 1 to 3 and 6 of article VII of the
  Agreement between Canada and the United States of America for
  Promotion of Safety on the Great Lakes by Means of Radio, 1973, if

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the vessel is engaged on a voyage in the Great
    Lakes Basin, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} article V of that Agreement requires the vessel to
    be in compliance; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a Safety Convention vessel, Part 3-3
  of section A-VIII/2 of the STCW Code.

#### [Composition of a Radio Watch]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731104 .Subheading}

- **[[266]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-266
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  the master of a vessel that is required to be fitted with a VHF
  radiotelephone installation shall ensure that a person who holds one
  of the following certificates referred to in Schedule II to the
  [Radiocommunication Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-96-484) is in
  charge of the radio watch:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Restricted Operator Certificate with Maritime
    Qualification;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} General Operator Certificate; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} Restricted Operator's Certificate (ROC-MC).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master of a vessel engaged on a voyage other than
  a sheltered waters voyage and that is required to be fitted with a VHF
  radiotelephone installation capable of digital selective calling shall
  ensure that the person in charge of the radio watch holds

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel that is engaged on a near
    coastal voyage, Class 2 and that has such a VHF installation as its
    only radiotelephone installation, a Restricted Operator Certificate
    with Maritime Qualifications issued after January 1, 2005; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in all other cases, a Restricted Operator's
    Certificate (ROC-MC) issued under the [Radiocommunication
    Act](/eng/acts/R-2).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel referred to
  in paragraph 207(1)(b) of the [Navigation Safety Regulations,
  2020](/eng/regulations/SOR-2020-216) that is operating in sea area A2,
  sea area A3 or sea area A4, shall employ and the master of that vessel
  shall ensure that there is on board

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a member of the complement who holds a First-class
    Radioelectronic certificate; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} at least two persons

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} each of whom holds a General Operator
      certificate, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} who, between them, are in charge of the radio
      watch for at least 16 hours during any 24-hour period.

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2020-216, s. 417](#1263800-1264135){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1263800-1264135.html"
  role="button"}

Version](section-266-20070701.html "Link to previous version of section 266"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Principal Communicator During an Emergency]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731118 .Subheading}

- **[[267]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-267 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel of at least 300 gross tonnage shall assign a crew
  member as principal communicator whose duty is to operate the
  radiocommunication equipment during an emergency.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The principal communicator of a vessel shall hold, at
  a minimum, the certificate referred to in section 266 that applies in
  respect of that vessel.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, owing to the duration of an emergency, the
  continuous presence of the principal communicator on the bridge is
  unreasonable, the master may assign any other member of the complement
  who is the holder of any of the certificates referred to in section
  266 to replace the principal communicator.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (1), if the continuous presence of
  the principal communicator on the bridge becomes unnecessary, the
  master may relieve them of their duty.

### [DIVISION 8]{.HLabel2}[Medical Examination of Seafarers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731124 .Subheading}

#### [Designation of Marine Medical Examiners]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731125 .Subheading}

**[[268]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-268 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Division, the Minister shall designate, as a marine medical
examiner, a physician who applies for that designation and who meets the
requirements of Chapter VI of the International Labour Organization and
World Health Organization publication entitled Guidelines for Conducting
Pre-sea and Periodic Medical Fitness Examination for Seafarers.

#### [Employment of Seafarers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731127 .Subheading}

- **[[269]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-269 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall employ as a seafarer a person to whom this Division
  applies unless the person produces a medical certificate issued by the
  Minister under section 278 or a provisional medical certificate issued
  under section 275.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The documents referred to in subsection (1) shall
  attest to the seafarer's ability to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} perform the duties for which they are to be
    employed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} complete the voyages to be engaged on by the vessel
    on board which they are to be employed.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person to whom this Division applies shall accept
  employment as a seafarer unless they hold a document referred to in
  subsection (1) that applies to their situation and that attests to the
  seafarer possessing the abilities referred to in subsection (2).

#### [Medical Fitness]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731134 .Subheading}

- **[[270]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-270
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (4),
  if a marine medical examiner, physician or registered nurse who, in
  conformity with section 272, conducts a medical examination of a
  seafarer, considers that the seafarer is fit for sea service, taking
  into account the medical standards set out in the International Labour
  Organization and World Health Organization publication entitled
  Guidelines for Conducting Pre-sea and Periodic Medical Fitness
  Examination for Seafarers, as well as the following medical standards,
  they shall issue to the seafarer a provisional medical certificate
  issued under section 275 declaring the seafarer fit for sea service,
  with or without limitations as they deem appropriate:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} adequate muscle strength to lift and carry a weight
    of 22 kg;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the physical capacity to wear breathing apparatus
    and the seafarer's personal life-saving equipment while climbing
    ladders;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the agility and strength to carry out the duties
    that may be assigned to them regarding fire fighting and vessel
    abandonment in an emergency; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the physical and mental fitness to meet the
    occupational and operational requirements of the position that they
    occupy or seek to occupy.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The medical standards for vision and hearing do not
  apply to an engineer who held an Engineer certificate before July 30,
  2002 if they began acquiring the amount of sea service required to
  obtain that certificate before July 30, 1997 and if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have obtained or are in the process of
    obtaining a higher ranking Engineer certificate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have exchanged, after July 30, 2002, a
    certificate issued before that date; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} they have obtained a continued proficiency
    certificate after July 30, 1997 but before the coming into force of
    this section.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The medical standards for colour vision do not apply
  to a seafarer who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is not required under these Regulations to hold a
    certificate of competency to perform their duties on board a vessel;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is required to hold one of the following
    certificates to perform their duties on board a vessel:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Engine-room Rating,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} Ship's Cook,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} Proficiency in Fast Rescue Boats,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} Proficiency in Survival Craft and Rescue Boats
      Other Than Fast Rescue Boats,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} Restricted Proficiency in Survival Craft and
      Rescue Boats Other Than Fast Rescue Boats,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} Oil and Chemical Tanker Familiarization,

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} Liquefied Gas Tanker Familiarization,

    - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} Passenger Safety Management,

    - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} Specialized Passenger Safety Management (Ro-Ro
      Vessels), or

    - [(x)]{.lawlabel} Compass Adjuster.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A seafarer is not required to meet the medical
  standards for visual acuity in each eye if they meet those standards
  when both eyes are tested together and if they

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are not required to hold a certificate to perform
    their duties on board a vessel under these Regulations and started
    to acquire sea service before July 30, 1997; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} held a certificate on July 30, 1997, and had begun
    to acquire the qualifying service for it before June 1, 1973, even
    if the certificate was exchanged or endorsed after July 29, 1997.

#### [Application for Examination]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731161 .Subheading}

**[[271]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-271 .sectionLabel}** An applicant for a
medical examination under this Division shall make an application to the
marine medical examiner, physician or registered nurse referred to in
section 272 in the form established by the Minister.

#### [Capacity to Conduct Medical Examination]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731163 .Subheading}

- **[[272]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-272
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), only a
  marine medical examiner may conduct a medical examination of and issue
  a provisional medical certificate to a seafarer to whom this Division
  applies under subsection 200(7).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any physician or registered nurse may conduct a
  medical examination and issue a provisional medical certificate to a
  seafarer if there is no marine medical examiner within 200 km of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the area of operation in Canadian waters of the
    vessel on board which the seafarer is employed or seeks to be
    employed; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the seafarer's place of residence.

#### [Medical Examination]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731169 .Subheading}

**[[273]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-273 .sectionLabel}** A marine medical
examiner, physician or registered nurse conducting a medical examination
under this Division shall ensure that the seafarer is assessed in
accordance with the requirements set out in section 270.

#### [Aids to Vision and Hearing]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731171 .Subheading}

**[[274]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-274 .sectionLabel}** A seafarer who is
required to use an aid to vision or hearing to meet the requirements set
out in section 270 shall

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in every case, use the aid to perform their duties on
  board a vessel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an aid to vision, possess at least two
  of them; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an aid to hearing, possess replacement
  batteries for it.

#### [Provisional Medical Certificates]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731176 .Subheading}

- **[[275]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-275
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} After completing the medical
  examination of a seafarer under this Division, a marine medical
  examiner, physician or registered nurse shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provide to the Minister

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} except in the case set out in paragraph (c), a
      copy of the provisional medical certificate that, subject to
      section 276, is in the form established by the Minister,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the original copy of the completed medical
      examination report form, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant medical report;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} except in the case set out in paragraph (c), issue
    a provisional medical certificate to the seafarer; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case where the seafarer is considered unfit
    for sea service, provide a provisional letter, addressed to the
    seafarer and the Minister and signed by the marine medical examiner,
    physician or registered nurse, as the case may be, attesting to
    their refusal to issue a provisional medical certificate and giving
    the reasons for determining that the seafarer is unfit for sea
    service.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The marine medical examiner, physician or registered
  nurse, as the case may be, shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} except in the case set out in paragraph (1)(c), set
    out in the provisional medical certificate their assessment of the
    seafarer's suitability as

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} fit for sea service without limitations, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} fit for sea service with limitations, as
      specified in the certificate; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if they issue a provisional medical certificate but
    remain uncertain as to the seafarer's medical fitness, request that
    the Minister take one or more of the actions set out in paragraphs
    278(2)(a) to (c) and issue a decision referred to in subsection
    278(4).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The validity period of a provisional medical
  certificate ends on the earliest of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date of issuance of a medical certificate by
    the Minister or the date of a letter from the Minister declaring the
    Minister's refusal to issue a medical certificate,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 6 months after the date of issuance of the
    provisional medical certificate if the certificate is not renewed,
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 1 year after the date of issuance of the
    provisional medical certificate if the certificate is renewed.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The provisional medical certificate referred to in
  subparagraph (2)(a)(ii) shall be accompanied by a supporting letter,
  addressed to the seafarer and the Minister and signed by the marine
  medical examiner, physician or registered nurse, as the case may be,
  giving the reasons for determining that the seafarer is fit for sea
  service with limitations.

**[[276]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-276 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Division, the Minister may accept a provisional medical certificate
with respect to a seafarer provided to the Minister by a marine industry
association following a medical examination performed, at the request of
the association, by a marine medical examiner, despite the fact that the
certificate is not in the form established by the Minister, but only if
it meets the requirements of section 275.

#### [Contestation of the Results of a Medical Examination]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731196 .Subheading}

- **[[277]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-277 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following persons may submit a memorandum to the Minister contesting a
  provisional medical certificate or a provisional letter of refusal to
  issue a provisional medical certificate with respect to a seafarer:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the seafarer's employer;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the seafarer's prospective employer; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the seafarer if they have been declared

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} unfit for sea service and have been refused a
      medical certificate, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} fit for sea service with limitations.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the provisional medical certificate declaring the
  holder to be fit for sea service or fit for sea service with
  limitations remains in force, a seafarer's employer or prospective
  employer who, taking into account the occupational and operational
  requirements of the position that the seafarer occupies or seeks to
  occupy, has grounds to believe that the seafarer's state of health
  might constitute a risk to the safety of the vessel on board which
  they occupy or seek to occupy a position or to the safety of other
  persons on board, may submit a memorandum to the Minister requesting
  that the Minister take at least one of the actions set out in
  subsection 278(2).

#### [Issuance of a Medical Certificate or Letter of Refusal to Issue One]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731205 .Subheading}

- **[[278]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-278 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  seafarer who has received a provisional medical certificate declaring
  them fit for sea service with limitations or a provisional letter of
  refusal to issue a provisional medical certificate, may, within 30
  days after receiving the document, request from the Minister a
  reconsideration of that decision.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} On receiving a request under subsection (1), the
  Minister shall take at least one of the following actions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} direct that further medical examinations or tests
    be carried out and, if the Minister wishes, stipulate the nature of
    the examinations or tests required and the persons or organizations
    to carry them out;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} consult any expert on the medical fitness of the
    seafarer or the occupational and operational requirements of the
    position that the seafarer occupies or could occupy if the seafarer
    had the required medical certificate; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} name a medical reconsideration committee that will
    function in accordance with the procedures set out in section 279
    and that will be responsible for giving the Minister its
    recommendations concerning a provisional medical certificate or a
    provisional letter of refusal to issue a provisional medical
    certificate, a copy of which was sent under section 275 or 276,
    which committee shall be comprised of the following persons acting
    as referees:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a physician who is independent of the shipowner
      and any organization of shipowners or seafarers,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a marine occupational expert who is independent
      of the shipowner and any organization of shipowners or seafarers
      and who holds a certificate of competency valid under these
      Regulations that would permit the expert to evaluate the
      occupational and operational requirements of the position that the
      seafarer occupies or seeks to occupy, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a member of the marine industry who is
      acceptable to the seafarer.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister receives a copy of a provisional
  medical certificate issued under section 275 or 276, a provisional
  letter of refusal to issue a provisional medical certificate or a
  memorandum submitted under section 277 and has reason to believe,
  taking into account the criteria set out in subsection (5), that the
  provisional medical certificate or the provisional letter of refusal
  to issue one is incomplete or erroneous, the Minister may take any of
  the actions set out in paragraphs (2)(a) to (c).

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} After having taken into consideration the provisional
  medical certificate or the provisional letter of refusal to issue a
  provisional medical certificate, the health of the seafarer to whom it
  was issued, any memorandum submitted under section 277 and any
  recommendation made by the persons referred to in paragraphs (2)(a) to
  (c), the Minister shall reconsider that certificate or letter in
  accordance with the criteria set out in subsection (5) and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} issue a Canadian maritime document in the form of a
    medical certificate declaring the seafarer, taking into account the
    specific requirements of subsection 270(1), as

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} fit for sea service without limitations,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} fit for sea service with limitations, as
      specified in the certificate; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} issue, sign and cause to be filed, a letter of
    refusal to issue a medical certificate, giving the reasons for
    determining that the seafarer is unfit for sea service and refusing
    to issue that Canadian maritime document.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister's decision with regard to any medical
  certificate shall be based on the following criteria:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the occupational and operational requirements of
    the position that the seafarer occupies or seeks to occupy;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the level of risk involved in the position referred
    to in paragraph (a) with regard to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the seafarer,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} other seafarers and, if applicable, the
      passengers on board the vessel on which the position exists,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the vessel on board which the position exists,
      and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the health and safety of the general public; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any relevant consideration linked to human rights
    as set out in the Canadian Charter of Rights and Freedoms and the
    [Canadian Bill of Rights](/eng/acts/C-12.3).

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} A medical certificate referred to in subparagraph
  (4)(a)(ii) shall be accompanied by a supporting letter, signed and
  placed on file, giving the reasons for determining that the seafarer
  is fit for sea service with limitations.

#### [Medical Reconsideration Procedure for a Provisional Medical Certificate]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731230 .Subheading}

- **[[279]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-279 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Chairperson of the medical reconsideration committee is the marine
  occupational expert, and all decisions of the committee are by a
  simple majority.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} After reconsidering the provisional medical
  certificate or the letter of refusal to issue one and the state of
  health of the seafarer to whom a certificate or letter was issued, the
  medical reconsideration committee may direct that further medical
  examinations or tests be carried out and may stipulate the nature of
  the examinations or tests and the persons or organizations to carry
  them out.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The medical reconsideration committee shall recommend
  to the Minister whether the Minister should issue, with regard to the
  seafarer, a medical certificate or a letter of refusal to issue a
  medical certificate.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The medical reconsideration committee shall base its
  recommendations on the following criteria:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the occupational and operational requirements of
    the position that the seafarer occupies or seeks to occupy;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the level of risk involved in the position referred
    to in paragraph (a) with regard to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the seafarer,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} other seafarers and, if applicable, the
      passengers on board the vessel on which the position exists,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the vessel on board which the position exists,
      and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the health and safety of the general public; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any relevant consideration linked to human rights
    as set out in the Canadian Charter of Rights and Freedoms and the
    [Canadian Bill of Rights](/eng/acts/C-12.3).

#### [Contestation of the Results of a Medical Certificate with Limitations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731243 .Subheading}

- **[[280]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-280 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Transportation Appeal Tribunal of Canada has jurisdiction over reviews
  and appeals with regard to any medical certificate declaring the
  seafarer fit for sea service with limitations.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A seafarer who is issued a medical certificate
  declaring them fit for sea service with limitations under subparagraph
  278(4)(a)(ii) may request a review of that certificate by the
  Tribunal.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The review procedure set out in subsections 16.1(2)
  to (5) of the Act applies to a review of a medical certificate
  declaring a seafarer fit for sea service with limitations.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A seafarer may appeal to the Tribunal a determination
  made under paragraph 16.1(5)(b) of the Act.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The appeal procedure set out in section 20.5 of the
  Act applies to an appeal by a seafarer of a decision made under
  subsection 16.1(5) of the Act.

#### [Medical Examination Costs]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731250 .Subheading}

- **[[281]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-281
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3)
  and any applicable collective bargaining agreement, a seafarer who has
  applied for a medical examination under section 271 shall bear the
  costs of any medical examination or test undergone that results in
  their receiving one of the following documents:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a provisional medical certificate;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a medical certificate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a provisional letter of refusal to issue a
    provisional medical certificate; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a letter of refusal to issue a medical certificate.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An employer or prospective employer who submits a
  memorandum under section 277 shall assume any related costs.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister has received a copy of a provisional
  medical certificate issued under section 275 or 276 or a provisional
  letter of refusal to issue a provisional medical certificate and has
  taken any of the actions set out in paragraphs 278(2)(b) and (c), the
  Minister shall assume any related costs.

**\[282 to 299 reserved\]**

:::: {#731260 .Schedule}
::: {}
## [SCHEDULE TO PART 2]{.scheduleLabel}[(Subsections 216(5) and 247(3))]{.OriginatingRef}[Equipment]{.scheduleTitleText}  {#h-731261 .scheduleLabel}
:::

The equipment referred to in subsections 216(5) and 247(3) is the
following:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a centralized conning position from which a proper
  lookout can be kept in all weather conditions and at which are located

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} helm, speed, whistle and general alarm controls,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a shaft revolution and direction indicator for
    each propeller shaft,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a propeller pitch indicator for each controllable
    pitch propeller,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} a rudder angle indicator for each independently
    controlled rudder,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} a compass or compass repeater,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} a radar display and its associated controls and
    plotting facilities,

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} a clock, and

  - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} a facility for the primary control and use of

    - [(A)]{.lawlabel} each radiotelephone on which a listening watch is
      kept by any person assigned to the deck watch, and

    - [(B)]{.lawlabel} the internal communications systems that are
      required to be fitted in the vessel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an automatic steering device that incorporates manual
  override;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an electronic two-way voice communication system that
  is operable in the absence of the vessel's main source of power and
  connects the conning position with

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the master's accommodation,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the chief engineer's accommodation,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} each berth in which a person assigned to a deck
    watch is berthed,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} each mess, recreation room or accommodation
    passageway, and

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} any position close to the main engine controls;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a fire detection and alarm system for each crew mess,
  recreation room, galley and accommodation passageway, except on
  vessels where an efficient fire patrol system is carried out in those
  spaces by persons who are not, at the time of the patrol, assigned to
  the deck watch;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} an automatic warning system that immediately
  indicates on the bridge the loss of the watertight integrity of any
  bow, side or stern door that is designed for the passage of vehicles;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} remote monitoring and control devices that

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} indicate the soundings of, or excessive levels in,
    all ballast tanks and bilges,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} control the pumping of all of the ballast tanks
    and bilges, unless this is done by persons who, at the time of the
    pumping, are not assigned to the deck watch, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} control the ventilation of cargo spaces, unless
    this is done by persons who, at the time of that operation, are not
    assigned to the deck watch;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} a sound-signalling appliance that can automatically
  sound the whistle signals required by the [Collision
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1416);

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} centralized controls and automatic monitoring of two
  independent systems of permanently installed navigation lights that
  comply with the [Collision
  Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1416); and

- [(i)]{.lawlabel} light lunch facilities.
