## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Maritime Labour Standards]{.HTitleText1} {#h-731290 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731291 .Subheading}

**[[300]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-300 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part.

[[Convention]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[Convention]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Maritime Labour
    Convention, 2006. ([Convention]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[hours of rest]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hours of rest]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means rest time outside hours
    of work and does not include short breaks. ([heures de
    repos]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[seafarer recruitment and placement service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[seafarer recruitment and placement
    service]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any service that is engaged in
    recruiting or placing persons for work on vessels. ([service de
    recrutement et de placement des gens de mer]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[shipowner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[shipowner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the registered owner of a vessel; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person, such as the manager, agent or
      bare-boat charterer of a vessel, who has assumed responsibility
      for the operation of a vessel from its registered owner and who,
      on assuming that responsibility, has agreed to take over the
      duties and responsibilities imposed on shipowners under the
      Convention. ([armateur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731299 .Subheading}

- **[[301]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-301
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided in this
  Part, this Part applies in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Canadian vessels everywhere and foreign vessels in
    Canadian waters; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} seafarer recruitment and placement services that
    recruit or place persons for work on

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} Canadian vessels, including fishing vessels, that
      are engaged on a near coastal voyage, Class 1 or an unlimited
      voyage, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} foreign vessels.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided in subparagraph (1)(b)(i) and
  sections 319, 334 and 335, this Part does not apply in respect of
  fishing vessels.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Except as provided in subsection (4), this Part does
  not apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} pleasure craft;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} vessels of traditional build, such as canoes and
    kayaks; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} vessels that are capable of engaging in the
    drilling for, or the production, conservation or processing of, oil
    or gas.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} This Part, other than sections 339 and 340, applies
  in respect of vessels that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are capable of engaging in the drilling for, or the
    production, conservation or processing of, oil or gas; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are engaged in navigation.

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Requirements Regarding Age]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731314 .Subheading}

#### [Minimum Age]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731315 .Subheading}

**[[302]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-302 .sectionLabel}** The master of a
Canadian vessel shall ensure that no person under 16 years of age is
employed, engaged or works on the vessel.

#### [Persons Under 18 Years of Age]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731317 .Subheading}

- **[[303]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-303 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a Canadian vessel that is engaged on an unlimited voyage, a
  near coastal voyage, Class 1 or an international voyage, other than an
  inland voyage, shall ensure that every person under 18 years of age
  who is employed, engaged or works on the vessel does not work during a
  period of at least nine consecutive hours that begins at or before
  midnight and ends at or after 5:00 a.m.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of a person
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the training of the person in accordance with an
    approved training program would be impaired; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the specific nature of the person's duties or an
    approved training program requires the person to work during that
    period and the work will not have a detrimental impact on the
    person's health or well-being.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Paragraph (2)(b) is not applicable if the Minister
  has determined, after consultation with the shipowners' and crew
  members' organizations concerned, if any, that the work will have a
  detrimental impact on the person's health or well-being.

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Seafarer Recruitment and Placement Services]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731324 .Subheading}

#### [Recruitment and Placement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731325 .Subheading}

- **[[304]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-304 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall operate a seafarer recruitment and placement service
  unless they hold a Seafarer Recruitment and Placement Service Licence
  issued by the Minister.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a vessel shall
  ensure that persons are not recruited or placed for work on the vessel
  by a seafarer recruitment and placement service unless the service

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if it is located in Canada, holds a Seafarer
    Recruitment and Placement Service Licence issued by the Minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if it is located in a foreign state that has
    ratified the Convention,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is a private service that complies with the laws
      of the state that implement Standard A1.4 of the Convention, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is a public service operated by the state; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if it is located in a foreign state that has not
    ratified the Convention, complies with the requirements set out in
    Standard A1.4.5(c)(i) to (vi) of the Convention and does not

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} impose, directly or indirectly, fees or other
      charges on any person for recruiting, placing or providing
      employment to them on board a vessel, other than the cost of
      obtaining a medical certificate, the person's record of sea
      service or a passport or any other similar personal travel
      document other than a visa, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} use means, mechanisms or lists intended to
      prevent or deter any person from gaining employment for which they
      are qualified on board a vessel.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of a seafarer
  recruitment and placement service operated by a trade union that has
  been certified under the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) by the
  Canada Industrial Relations Board as the bargaining agent for the
  employees in a bargaining unit and the certification of which has not
  been revoked.

#### [Issuance of Licences]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731337 .Subheading}

**[[305]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-305 .sectionLabel}** On application, the
Minister shall issue a Seafarer Recruitment and Placement Service
Licence if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has procedures in place to ensure
  compliance with Part 1 of the [Personal Information Protection and
  Electronic Documents Act](/eng/acts/P-8.6);

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicant's staff responsible for the supervision
  of public or private seafarer recruitment and placement services for a
  vessel's crew with responsibility for the vessel's safe navigation and
  pollution prevention operations have had training in those operations,
  including the sea-service experience required to obtain a certificate
  under Part 1, and have knowledge of the maritime industry, including
  the STCW Convention and the maritime labour conventions and
  recommendations published by the International Labour Organization;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has a system of quality standards in
  place; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the applicant has insurance or other financial
  arrangements sufficient to compensate crew members for monetary loss
  that they may reasonably incur as a result of a failure of the
  applicant, the authorized representative of a Canadian vessel or the
  shipowner, in the case of a foreign vessel, to meet its obligations to
  the crew members under their contracts of employment.

#### [Licensees]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731343 .Subheading}

- **[[306]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-306
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every licensee shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} maintain on their premises an up-to-date register
    of all persons they recruit or place;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} ensure that every person recruited or placed by
    them is qualified and holds the documents necessary for the position
    concerned, and that the person's contract of employment is in
    accordance with the applicable laws and regulations and any
    applicable collective agreement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} ensure that every person recruited or placed by
    them is informed of their rights and obligations under their
    contract of employment before or in the process of engagement and
    that proper arrangements are made for them to examine their contract
    of employment before and after they are signed and for them to
    receive a copy of the contract;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} ensure, as far as feasible, that the authorized
    representative of a Canadian vessel or the shipowner, in the case of
    a foreign vessel, has the means to protect persons that the licensee
    recruits or places for work on the vessel from being stranded in a
    foreign port; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} examine and respond to any complaint concerning
    their activities and advise the Minister of any unresolved
    complaint.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No licensee shall use means, mechanisms or lists
  intended to prevent or deter any person from gaining employment for
  which they are qualified on board a vessel.

#### [Fees or Other Charges]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731352 .Subheading}

**[[307]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-307 .sectionLabel}** No person shall impose,
directly or indirectly, fees or other charges on any person for
recruiting, placing or providing employment to them on board a vessel,
other than the cost of obtaining a medical certificate, the person's
record of sea service or a passport or any other similar personal travel
document other than a visa.

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Conditions of Employment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731354 .Subheading}

#### [Articles of Agreement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731355 .Subheading}

- **[[308]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-308 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of subsection 91(1) of the Act, the master of a Canadian
  vessel shall enter into articles of agreement if the vessel

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} engages on unlimited voyages or international
    voyages, other than inland voyages; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is of 100 gross tonnage or more and engages on near
    coastal voyages, Class 1, other than inland voyages.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In addition to the information required by subsection
  91(2) of the Act, articles of agreement must contain the following
  information:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the crew member's date and place of birth;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the authorized representative's name and address;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the place at which and the date on which the
    articles of agreement were entered into;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the capacity in which the crew member is to be
    employed;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the crew member's wages or the
    formula for calculating them if they are calculated using a formula;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the crew member's paid annual leave
    or the formula for calculating it if it is calculated using a
    formula; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} whether a collective agreement applies to the crew
    member.

#### [Collective Agreements That Are Part of Articles of Agreement]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731368 .Subheading}

**[[309]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-309 .sectionLabel}** If a collective
agreement is part of the articles of agreement of a crew member of a
Canadian vessel of 100 gross tonnage or more that is engaged on an
unlimited voyage, a near coastal voyage, Class 1 or an international
voyage, other than an inland voyage, the vessel's master shall ensure
that a copy of the agreement is available on board and, if the copy in
not in English, that a copy in English is available on board.

#### [Visas]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731370 .Subheading}

**[[310]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-310 .sectionLabel}** The authorized
representative of a Canadian vessel and the shipowner, in the case of a
foreign vessel, shall pay for the visas of crew members on board.

#### [Termination of Employment and Payment of Wages and Compensation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731372 .Subheading}

##### [Application]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731373 .Subheading}

**[[311]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-311 .sectionLabel}** Sections 312 to 318
apply in respect of Canadian vessels that are engaged on an unlimited
voyage, a near coastal voyage, Class 1 or an international voyage, other
than an inland voyage.

##### [Termination of Employment by Employer]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731375 .Subheading}

- **[[312]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-312
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any collective agreement
  that applies to the crew member, the master of a vessel who intends to
  terminate a crew member's employment shall give the crew member

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} notice in writing, at least one week before the
    date specified in the notice, of the intention to terminate their
    employment on that date; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an indemnity equal to one week's wages at their
    regular rate of pay for their regular hours of work, in lieu of the
    notice.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 230 of the [Canada Labour
    Code](/eng/acts/L-2) applies;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the crew member's employment is terminated for
    committing a serious violation of their contract of employment; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the master and the crew member agree on a shorter
    notice of termination.

##### [Termination of Employment by Crew Member]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731384 .Subheading}

- **[[313]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-313
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any collective agreement
  that applies to the crew member, a crew member employed on a vessel
  shall give the vessel's master at least one week's notice of the crew
  member's intention to terminate their employment.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the crew member is unable by reason of illness to
    perform their duties; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the master and the crew member agree on a shorter
    notice of termination.

##### [Loss of Vessel or Death]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731390 .Subheading}

**[[314]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-314 .sectionLabel}** A crew member's
employment on a vessel is terminated

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on their death; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel is shipwrecked or otherwise totally
  unseaworthy.

##### [Monthly Payment and Accounting]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731394 .Subheading}

- **[[315]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-315 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel shall ensure that crew members' wages are paid

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} monthly or at more frequent regular intervals; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with any applicable collective
    agreement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master shall give every crew member a monthly
  account of their wages due and the amounts paid, including the rate of
  exchange used if payment is made in currency or at a rate different
  from the one agreed to.

##### [Payment on Termination of Employment]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731400 .Subheading}

**[[316]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-316 .sectionLabel}** The master of a vessel
shall ensure that a crew member is paid any wages due when their
employment is terminated

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} without undue delay; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with any applicable collective
  agreement.

##### [Transmittal of Wages]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731404 .Subheading}

- **[[317]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-317 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a vessel shall take measures to provide
  crew members with a means to transmit all or part of their wages to
  their families, dependants, assigns or successors. The measures may
  include a system to enable crew members, when they enter into articles
  of agreement or while working on board, to allot a portion of their
  earnings to their families by bank transfers or similar means.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative shall ensure that the
  allotments are remitted in due time and directly to the person or
  persons nominated by the crew members.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative shall ensure that any
  charge for the measures taken under subsection (1) is reasonable and
  that the rate of exchange is at the prevailing market rate or is
  determined in accordance with any applicable collective agreement.

##### [Compensation of Crew Members in Case of Shipwrecks]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731409 .Subheading}

- **[[318]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-318 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a vessel that is shipwrecked shall pay to
  every crew member who was on board immediately before the shipwreck an
  indemnity against unemployment resulting from the shipwreck.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The indemnity shall be paid for the days during which
  the crew member remains unemployed at the same rate as the wages
  payable under the contract of employment, but the total indemnity
  payable to any one seafarer may be limited to two months' wages.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Crew members shall have the same legal remedies for
  recovering the indemnities as they have for recovering arrears of
  wages.

#### [Hours of Work and Hours of Rest]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731414 .Subheading}

##### [Application]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731415 .Subheading}

- **[[319]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-319
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Sections 320, 322 and 323 apply in
  respect of Canadian vessels, including fishing vessels of 100 gross
  tonnage or more,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} engaged on sheltered waters voyages; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} engaged on near coastal voyages, Class 1 or near
    coastal voyages, Class 2 while the vessels are in any waters other
    than those of a foreign state that has ratified the Convention.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sections 321 to 324 apply in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Canadian vessels

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} engaged on near coastal voyages, Class 1 or near
      coastal voyages, Class 2 while the vessels are in the waters of a
      foreign state that has ratified the Convention, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} engaged on unlimited voyages; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} foreign vessels in Canadian waters.

##### [Minimum and Maximum Periods]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731425 .Subheading}

**[[320]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-320 .sectionLabel}** The master of a vessel
referred to in subsection 319(1) shall ensure that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the master and every crew member have

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} at least six consecutive hours of rest in every
    24-hour period, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} at least 16 hours of rest in every 48-hour period;
    and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} not more than 18 hours but not less than six hours
  elapse between the end of a rest period and the beginning of the next
  rest period.

<!-- -->

- **[[321]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-321 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel referred to in subsection 319(2) shall ensure that
  the master and every crew member

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} do not work more than 14 hours in any 24-hour
    period or more than 72 hours in any 7-day period; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have at least 10 hours of rest in every 24-hour
    period and 77 hours of rest in every 7-day period.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master shall ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the hours of rest are divided into no more than two
    periods, one of which is at least 6 hours in length; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the interval between two consecutive rest periods
    does not exceed 14 hours.

##### [Other Considerations and Limitations]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731438 .Subheading}

- **[[322]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-322 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel shall ensure that the danger posed by the fatigue
  of crew members, especially those whose duties involve navigational
  safety and the safe and secure operation of the vessel, is taken into
  account when determining the scheduled hours of work and rest.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Despite sections 320 and 321 and subject to
  subsection (1), the master may ensure that the hours of work and rest
  are in accordance with an applicable collective agreement that
  provides for hours of work and rest that are no less favourable to
  crew members.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Sections 320 and 321 and subsection (2) do not apply
  when the master is conducting practice musters, fire-fighting drills
  or survival craft drills in accordance with regulations made under the
  Act if the master does so in a manner that minimizes the disturbance
  of rest periods and does not induce fatigue.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Sections 320 and 321 and subsection (2) do not apply
  in respect of a crew member who is on call if the crew member has
  compensatory rest periods and the rest period required by those
  provisions is disturbed by calls to work.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The master may suspend the schedule of hours of work
  and rest if it is necessary to do so for the immediate safety of the
  vessel, persons on board or the cargo, or for the purposes of giving
  assistance to other vessels or persons in distress at sea. As soon as
  feasible, the master shall ensure that every crew member who has
  performed work in a scheduled rest period is provided with a
  compensatory rest period.

##### [Records]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731445 .Subheading}

**[[323]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-323 .sectionLabel}** The master of a vessel
shall keep a record of every crew member's daily hours of work or hours
of rest until the crew member is discharged.

##### [Table of Shipboard Working Arrangements]{.HTitleText4} {#h-731447 .Subheading}

- **[[324]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-324 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a vessel shall ensure that a table with the shipboard
  working arrangements is posted in a conspicuous place on board the
  vessel. For every position on board, the table shall contain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the schedule of service at sea and in port; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the maximum hours of work or the minimum hours of
    rest required by section 321 or any applicable collective agreement.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The table shall be in the working language of the
  vessel and in English.

#### [Annual Leave]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731453 .Subheading}

- **[[325]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-325 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  section applies in respect of Canadian vessels that are engaged on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} near coastal voyages, Class 1 or international
    voyages that are not in Canadian waters or the waters of the
    continental United States (including Alaska); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} unlimited voyages.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Unless the time for a crew member to take paid annual
  leave is fixed by the applicable collective agreement or an
  arbitration award, the vessel's authorized representative shall
  determine when it is to be taken after consultation and, as far as
  possible, in agreement with the crew member or their representative.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a crew member is required to take their paid
  annual leave from a place other than the place at which they first
  came on board, the vessel's authorized representative shall ensure
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they are entitled to free transportation to the
    latter place, including the subsistence and other costs directly
    involved in their return; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the travel time involved is not deducted from their
    paid annual leave.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative shall ensure that no
  crew member who is on paid annual leave is recalled unless there is an
  extreme emergency.

#### [Shore Leave]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731463 .Subheading}

**[[326]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-326 .sectionLabel}** The master of a
Canadian vessel shall grant crew members shore leave consistent with
their health and well-being and with the operational requirements of
their positions.

#### [Repatriation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731465 .Subheading}

- **[[327]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-327 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  authorized representative of a Canadian vessel shall ensure that no
  crew member is required to make an advance payment at the beginning of
  their employment towards the expenses referred to in subsection 94(1)
  of the Act or section 328.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative shall ensure that the
  time a crew member spends waiting to be returned and being returned
  under subsection 94(1) of the Act or section 328 is not deducted from
  the paid leave accrued to them.

<!-- -->

- **[[328]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-328
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except in the case of desertion or
  mutual agreement, before a Canadian vessel is disposed of or is
  transferred to the flag of a foreign state or when a Canadian vessel
  is totally unseaworthy, the vessel's authorized representative shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ensure that arrangements are made to return every
    crew member to the place where they first came on board or to
    another place to which they have agreed; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} pay the expenses of returning every crew member as
    well as all expenses, including medical expenses, that the crew
    member reasonably incurs before being returned.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of a Canadian vessel
  shall have insurance or other financial arrangements sufficient to
  compensate crew members for any monetary loss that they may reasonably
  incur as a result of a failure of the authorized representative to
  meet its obligations to them under subsection 94(1) of the Act or
  subsection (1).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the authorized representative does not comply with
  subsection (1), the Minister may act in place of the authorized
  representative and any expenses incurred by the Minister constitute a
  debt due to Her Majesty in right of Canada by the authorized
  representative and may be recovered as such in a court of competent
  jurisdiction.

#### [Food and Water]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731475 .Subheading}

- **[[329]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-329 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a Canadian vessel that is engaged on a voyage other than a
  sheltered waters voyage, an inland voyage or an intraprovincial voyage
  shall ensure that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the crew members who are living on board can meet
    the recommendations of Eating Well with Canada's Food Guide;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is made available sufficient potable water
    for all crew members on board; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's potable water system meets the
    requirements set out in paragraphs 8(a) to (g) of the [Potable Water
    Regulations for Common Carriers](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1105).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall charge a crew member for food or
  water required to comply with subsection (1).

#### [Obligation of Persons Who Provide Crew Members]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731482 .Subheading}

**[[330]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-330 .sectionLabel}** If the authorized
representative of a Canadian vessel entered into an agreement with
another person to provide crew members, that other person shall, in lieu
of the authorized representative or the master with respect to those
crew members, comply with the obligation of the authorized
representative or master set out in

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} section 310, in respect of visas required to join the
  vessel;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} subsection 327(1); and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} subsections 328(1) and (2).

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Separate Hospital Accommodation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731487 .Subheading}

- **[[331]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-331
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s.
  527\]]{.Repealed}

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a vessel has separate hospital accommodation on
  board, the master shall ensure that the accommodation is used
  exclusively for medical purposes.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of vessels
  constructed before the day on which the Convention comes into force in
  Canada.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of this section, a vessel is
  constructed on the earlier of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which its keel is laid, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which construction identifiable with a
    specific vessel begins.

- [SOR/2023-257, s. 527](#1431514-1435917){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431514-1435917.html"
  role="button"}
:::

Version](section-331-20070701.html "Link to previous version of section 331"){.PITLink}

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[On-board Complaint Procedures]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731495 .Subheading}

- **[[332]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-332 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a Canadian vessel that is engaged on an unlimited voyage, a
  near coastal voyage, Class 1 or an international voyage, other than an
  inland voyage, shall ensure that the crew members can avail themselves
  of the on-board complaint procedures set out in this section.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Crew members may make a complaint with respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an alleged breach of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any of the applicable requirements set out in
      subsection 93(1) or 94(1) of the Act or in subsection 334(1),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} section 425 of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or the right to freedom of association and
      to collective bargaining set out in Part I of the [Canada Labour
      Code](/eng/acts/L-2), or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} section 423 of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46) in respect of compelling someone to work; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an alleged discriminatory practice described in

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any of sections 7 to 12 and paragraph 14(1)(c) of
      the [Canadian Human Rights Act](/eng/acts/H-6), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} section 14.1 of that Act in respect of a
      complaint related to an alleged discriminatory practice described
      in a provision referred to in subparagraph (i).

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The complaint may be made to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the head of the crew member's department;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the crew member's superior officer;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's master; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's authorized representative.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} A complainant may be represented by any other crew
  member on board if that crew member consents.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The complainant and their representative may attend
  any meeting or hearing with respect to the complaint.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} If a person referred to in paragraph (3)(a) or (b)
  cannot resolve a complaint to the satisfaction of the complainant, the
  person shall refer it to the vessel's master.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} If the master cannot resolve a complaint to the
  satisfaction of the complainant, the master shall refer it to the
  vessel's authorized representative.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} The person to whom a complaint is made or referred
  shall attempt to resolve it as soon as feasible.

- [(9)]{.lawlabel} The person who resolves a complaint shall record in
  writing the details of the complaint and its resolution and give a
  copy of the record to the complainant and any other parties involved.

- [(10)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section shall be construed as
  limiting or restricting any right a crew member may have under any
  other law or under any custom, contract or arrangement.

- [(11)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of complaints
  to which section 127.1 of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2)
  applies.

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[Maritime Labour Certificates and Declarations of Compliance]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731519 .Subheading}

#### [Requirements to Hold]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731520 .Subheading}

- **[[333]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-333
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every Canadian vessel of 500 gross
  tonnage or more that is engaged on an international voyage, other than
  an inland voyage, shall hold a Maritime Labour Certificate or an
  Interim Maritime Labour Certificate.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative of every Canadian
  vessel of 500 gross tonnage or more that is engaged on an
  international voyage, other than an inland voyage, shall hold a
  Declaration of Maritime Labour Compliance.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply in respect of a vessel
  that holds an Interim Maritime Labour Certificate.

#### [Issuance of Maritime Labour Certificates]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731525 .Subheading}

- **[[334]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-334 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application, the Minister shall issue a Maritime Labour Certificate to
  a Canadian vessel if the applicable requirements regarding the
  following are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the minimum age of crew members as set out in
    section 302 and the work hours of persons under 18 years of age as
    set out in section 303;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} medical certification as set out in Division 8 of
    Part 2;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} qualifications of seafarers as set out in Part 1;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} articles of agreement as set out in section 91 of
    the Act and section 308, collective agreements as set out in section
    309 and certificates of discharge as set out in section 92 of the
    Act;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} use of recruitment and placement services as set
    out in subsection 304(2);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} hours of work or rest as set out in sections 319 to
    323;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} crewing levels for the vessel as set out in Part 2;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} health and safety as set out in Part II of the
    [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2), as well as

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} accommodation and on-board recreational
      facilities as set out in the [Vessel Construction and Equipment
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2023-257),

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, SOR/2023-257, s. 528\]]{.Repealed}

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} food and catering as set out in section 329,
      and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} accident prevention as set out in the [Tackle
      Regulations](/eng/regulations/C.R.C.,_c._1494);

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} on-board complaint procedures as set out in section
    127.1 of the [Canada Labour Code](/eng/acts/L-2) and section 332;
    and

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} payment and transmittal of wages as set out in
    sections 315 to 317.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} On application, the Minister shall issue an Interim
  Maritime Labour Certificate to a Canadian vessel if the requirements
  referred to in subsection (3) are met and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel has just been registered in Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is a new authorized representative of the
    vessel following a change in ownership of the vessel.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The requirements referred to in subsection (2) are
  the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the applicable requirements referred to in
    paragraphs (1)(b) to (d);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the applicable requirements referred to in
    paragraphs (1)(a) and (e) to (j) in so far as meeting those
    requirements when the Interim Maritime Labour Certificate is issued
    is reasonable and feasible; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's master is familiar with the
    requirements referred to in subsection (1) and who is to comply with
    them.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1) to (3) apply in respect of fishing
  vessels that are Canadian vessels but they shall be considered as
  vessels that are not fishing vessels for the purpose of determining
  the applicable requirements referred to in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} paragraphs (1)(a), (b), (d), (e) and (h) to (j);
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} paragraph (1)(f) in so far as it relates to vessels
    referred to in subsection 319(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [SOR/2023-257, s. 528](#1431518-1435921){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1431518-1435921.html"

[Previous
Version](section-334-20130820.html "Link to previous version of section 334"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Issuance of Maritime Labour Declarations of Compliance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731552 .Subheading}

- **[[335]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-335 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application, the Minister shall issue a Declaration of Maritime Labour
  Compliance to the authorized representative of a Canadian vessel who
  has procedures in place to ensure compliance with the applicable
  requirements referred to in subsection 334(1).

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies in respect of fishing vessels
  that are Canadian vessels but they shall be considered as vessels that
  are not fishing vessels for the purpose of determining the applicable
  requirements referred to in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} paragraphs 334(1)(a), (b), (d), (e) and (h) to (j);
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} paragraph 334(1)(f) in so far as it relates to
    vessels referred to in subsection 319(2).

#### [Availability]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731558 .Subheading}

- **[[336]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-336 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of every Canadian vessel that holds a Maritime Labour
  Certificate shall keep the Certificate on board with a copy of the
  Declaration of Maritime Labour Compliance issued in respect of the
  vessel attached.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master of every Canadian vessel that holds an
  Interim Maritime Labour Certificate shall keep the Certificate on
  board.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The vessel's master shall ensure that the documents
  referred to in subsection (1) or (2), as the case may be,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are posted on the vessel's notice board for the
    information of the crew members; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are made available to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} crew members and their representatives, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} port state control officers in foreign states.

#### [Endorsements of Maritime Labour Certificates]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731567 .Subheading}

**[[337]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-337 .sectionLabel}** The authorized
representative of a Canadian vessel that holds a Maritime Labour
Certificate shall ensure that the certificate is endorsed as required by
Standard A5.1.3.2 of the Convention.

#### [Records of Inspections]{.HTitleText3} {#h-731569 .Subheading}

- **[[338]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-338 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a Canadian vessel that holds a Maritime Labour Certificate
  shall ensure that records of the results of inspections carried out to
  ensure compliance with any of the applicable requirements referred to
  in subsection 334(1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} are attached to the Declaration of Maritime Labour
    Compliance issued in respect of the vessel; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} are kept on board in electronic form and made
    available in that form to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} crew members and their representatives, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} port state control officers in foreign states.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master shall provide a copy of the records to
  crew members on request.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the records are not in English, the master shall
  ensure that an English translation accompanies the records or the
  copy, as the case may be.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of inspections
  carried out for the purpose of issuing a Maritime Labour Certificate
  or an Interim Maritime Labour Certificate.

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[Log Books]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731579 .Subheading}

- **[[339]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-339 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  master of a Canadian vessel of 100 gross tonnage or more that is
  engaged on an unlimited voyage, a near coastal voyage, Class 1 or an
  international voyage, other than an inland voyage, shall keep an
  official log book in a form approved by the Minister.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall approve forms of official log
  books so that the forms contain the spaces necessary for the entries
  required by section 340.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The official log book may be kept distinct from or
  united with the vessel's deck log book.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The master shall ensure that every entry required by
  section 340

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is made as soon as possible;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is dated to show the date of the event and of the
    entry if the entry is in respect of an event; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} is made not more than 24 hours after the arrival of
    the vessel at its final place of discharge if the entry is in
    respect of an event that happens before that arrival.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The master shall sign every entry in the official log
  book and shall ensure that every entry is also signed by the chief
  mate or another crew member and, if it is an entry in respect of an
  injury or a death, by the medical doctor on board, if there is one.

<!-- -->

- **[[340]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-340 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  official log book shall contain the following entries:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vessel's name, official number, port of
    registration and registered gross tonnage and net tonnage;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the master's name and certificate number;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the place at which and the date on which the voyage
    starts, the classification of the voyage and the place at which and
    the date on which the voyage ends;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a listing of the crew members on board;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} details of every injury that a crew member
    sustains, including the nature of the injury and the medical
    treatment adopted, if any;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} details of every birth of a child that occurs on
    board, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the child's date of birth,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the child's given names (if any), surname and
      gender,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the given names, surname, maiden name (if any),
      nationality and most recent place of residence of the child's
      mother, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the given names, surname, nationality and most
      recent place of residence of the child's father, if known;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} details of every death that occurs on board,
    including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the date of death,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the person's given names (if any), surname and
      gender,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the person's age,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the person's rank or occupation if the person
      was a crew member,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the person's nationality and most recent place of
      residence, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the cause of death;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the details of the wages due to any crew member who
    dies during a voyage, including the gross amount of all deductions
    to be made from those wages;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} details respecting every accident or incident
    associated with the vessel;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} details respecting every instance of a crew member
    deserting the vessel or committing a serious violation of their
    contract of employment; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} if a notice containing particulars of the vessel's
    draught and freeboard is required to be posted under section 12 of
    the [Load Line Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2007-99), the date
    and time the notice is posted.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the vessel is engaged on an international voyage,
  other than an inland voyage, the official log book shall also contain
  the following entries:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel holds a Local Load Line Certificate,
    an International Load Line Certificate or an International Load Line
    Exemption Certificate and leaves a place for the purpose of
    proceeding to sea,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the place from which the vessel departs,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the date and hour of departure,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the actual draught of water forward and aft,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the actual freeboard amidships on the port and
      starboard sides of the vessel and the average of those freeboards,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the density of the water,

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the adjustments to the applicable load line for
      the density of water and for the weight of fuel and all other
      materials required for consumption between the point of departure
      and the sea and the total of those adjustments, and

    - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} the mean draught and the mean freeboard
      amidships in saltwater as calculated after the adjustments
      referred to in subparagraph (vi) are made;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the vessel holds a Local Load Line Certificate,
    an International Load Line Certificate or an International Load Line
    Exemption Certificate, the positions of the deck line and load lines
    indicated on the certificate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the information required to be kept by subsection
    24(1) of the [Boat and Fire Drill and Means of Exit
    Regulations](/eng/regulations/SOR-2005-280); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a daily record of whether radio conditions and the
    state of the vessel's radio equipment are satisfactory or
    unsatisfactory.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The master shall send the information required by
  paragraphs (1)(a) to (c) to the Minister at the end of the voyage or
  when the master leaves the vessel, but in any event no later than when
  the registration of the vessel changes or the vessel is shipwrecked or
  abandoned.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The master shall provide each of the vessel's
  completed official log books to the vessel's authorized
  representative.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The authorized representative shall ensure that each
  of the vessel's official log books is kept until the earlier of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} five years after the day on which the log book was
    completed, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which a change is made in registration
    of the vessel.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} On request, the authorized representative shall
  provide the vessel's official log books to the Minister.

**\[341 to 399 reserved\]**

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[Repeals and Coming into Force]{.HTitleText1} {#h-731631 .Part}

### [Repeals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731632 .Subheading}

**[[400]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-400 .sectionLabel}** \[Repeal\]

**[[401]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-401 .sectionLabel}** \[Repeal\]

**[[402]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-402 .sectionLabel}** \[Repeal\]

### [Coming into Force]{.HTitleText2} {#h-731636 .Subheading}

- **[[Footnote
  ]{.wb-invisible}^\*^](#fnstar_e){#fnstar_e-ID0EBDA}[[403]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-403
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} These Regulations, except sections
  304 to 307 and 333 to 338, come into force on the day on which section
  2 of the [Canada Shipping Act, 2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15), chapter 26 of
  the Statutes of Canada, 2001, comes into force.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sections 304 to 307 and 333 to 338 come into force on
  the later of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day on which the Maritime Labour Convention,
    2006 comes into force in Canada, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day on which section 2 of the [Canada Shipping
    Act, 2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15), chapter 26 of the Statutes of Canada,
    2001, comes into force.
